Here, we experimentally evaluate two passive polarization compensation techniques: a free-space Quarter-Half-Quarter (QHQ) waveplate configuration and an in-fiber three-paddle Fiber Polarization Controller (FPC). The polarization transformation along each downconverted-photon path is independently compensated through a systematic path-by-path optimization procedure. Using both techniques, we recover high-quality polarization correlations and entanglement, with visibilities exceeding $93\%$ in three mutually unbiased bases and fidelities above $94.5\%$. The results demonstrate comparable restoration using free-space waveplate-based and fiber-based control, establishing a systematic framework for laboratory and short-reach quantum communication links.

</details>


### [64] [Optimal Fusion Strategies for Quantum Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02559)
*Kenneth Goodenough,Andrew Landahl,Joon Lee,Antonio Russo,Kevin Thompson*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper characterizes perfect fusion strategies for quantum error correction, revealing their properties and applicability in quantum computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To optimize performance in quantum error correction and quantum computation by finding effective fusion strategies for probabilistic qubit fusions in photonic systems.

Method: Complete characterization for cases with $k=1$ qubits, examining codes and fusion strategies that ensure all but one physical fusion can fail, leading to perfect fusion strategies.

Result: Recovery of existing perfect fusion strategies and the introduction of new ones for quantum parity-check codes; demonstration of genericity in random graph codes having perfect fusion strategies.

Conclusion: The study reveals a new graph parameter with practical significance, enhancing understanding of fusion strategies in quantum computation.

Abstract: Logical fusions are important for a number of tasks in quantum information, such as quantum error correction and quantum repeaters. In the photonic setting one must contend with the fact that physical fusions are probabilistic (i.e.~the associated qubits are measured in product bases), which---depending on the failures and the associated bases---can lead to a failure on the logical level. The choice of failure basis of each qubit is known as a fusion strategy, and finding good fusion strategies is important for optimizing performance of fusion-based quantum computation. Here we provide a complete characterization when $k=1$ qubits are encoded, and in particular characterize those codes and fusion strategies such that all but one physical fusion can fail, i.e.~\emph{perfect fusion strategies}. In doing so, we recover previously known perfect fusion strategies, and find perfect fusion strategies for quantum parity-check codes, answering an open question. We furthermore show that perfect fusion strategies are generic: random $[[n, 1, d]]$ graph codes admit a perfect fusion strategy with probability exponentially close to $1$. Additionally we motivate the study of a new graph parameter, namely the maximum degree of a graph at a given vertex taken over all LC-equivalent graphs, by giving a new operationally meaningful interpretation of it.

</details>


### [65] [Combinatorial optimization of connected UAV communication bridges for emergency response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02562)
*Matteo Vandelli,Daniele Dragoni*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a combinatorial optimization framework for deploying UAVs with 5G antennas for disaster response, using integer quadratic programming and exploring quantum computing as an alternative for scalability issues in time-sensitive scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the need for efficient UAV deployment in disaster scenarios to ensure signal coverage, minimize interference, and maintain network connectivity, focusing on real-time operational constraints.

Method: The authors model the problem as an integer quadratic program (IQP) and leverage CPLEX solver for numerical solutions, also exploring quantum computing by reformulating the problem as a QUBO problem for potential scalability improvement.

Result: Findings indicate that Time-to-Solution (TTS) increases exponentially with more sites, but approximate suboptimal solutions maintain connectivity, demonstrating a balance between solution quality and computational time. Quantum computing shows promise for small-scale problem instances.

Conclusion: This study highlights the critical challenge of optimizing UAV deployment under time constraints and suggests quantum algorithms as a viable approach to enhance solution capabilities for larger, more urgent disaster scenarios.

Abstract: We present a combinatorial optimization problem for the strategic deployment of UAVs equipped with 5G antennas to assist rescue operations in regions hit by natural disasters. Our goal is to optimize the placement of UAVs to provide coverage in flying ad-hoc networks among given candidate sites. Our formulation aims to maximize signal coverage and minimize interference while ensuring network connectivity. To mitigate interference effects, we incorporate the use of multiple frequencies. We formulate this problem as an integer quadratic program (IQP). We present numerical solutions obtained via the CPLEX solver and conduct a preliminary analysis of the problem's scalability in realistic network configurations. Our findings reveal a significant exponential increase in Time-to-Solution (TTS) as the number of sites grows, which poses a critical challenge in urgent, time-sensitive scenarios. To address this issue, approximate suboptimal solutions can be produced by enforcing a time limit on the solver. Although these solutions are not optimal, they preserve connectivity in most cases, providing a practical trade-off between solution quality and computational times that remain within feasible limits for real-time UAV redeployment. Recognizing the limitations of classical solvers in these contexts, we explore quantum computing as a promising alternative. Specifically, we reformulate the problem as a quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) problem, suitable for most quantum algorithms. Through high-performance computing emulation, we show that the quantum adiabatic algorithm (QAA) can accurately solve small-scale instances, paving the way for future application of quantum computing to large-scale, time-critical optimization problems in disaster response.

</details>


### [66] [Thouless pumping and generation of squeezed Fock-state superpositions in a Fock-state lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02569)
*Ruo Kun Cai,Ling Lin,Chun Wang Wu,Zhi Jiao Deng,Ping Xing Chen*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores Thouless pumping in a one-dimensional semi-infinite Fock-state lattice, focusing on the unique $rac{1}{	ext{sqrt}n}$ coupling and the implications for quantum state engineering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the potential of Thouless pumping in Fock-state lattices, with an aim to utilize topological transport for preparing non-classical states.

Method: Numerical evaluation of topological invariants and quantized dynamics in Fock-state basis, extension to squeezed Fock-state basis via inter-cell coupling and squeezing transformation.

Result: Establishment of a topologically protected scheme for preparing superpositions of squeezed Fock states, demonstrating the practical utility of Thouless pumping.

Conclusion: Thouless pumping in Fock-state lattices is presented as a novel approach for quantum state engineering, emphasizing its role in the preparation of non-classical bosonic states.

Abstract: In this paper, Thouless pumping in a one-dimensional semi-infinite Fock-state lattice is investigated. A distinctive feature of such lattices is the intrinsic $\sqrt{n}$-dependent coupling arising from the bosonic mode, which leads to spatially nonuniform hopping amplitudes. In the dimer limit, the topological invariants and the quantized transport dynamics in the Fock-state basis are numerically evaluated and analyzed. By introducing an additional inter-cell coupling and applying a squeezing transformation, the framework is then extended to Thouless pumping in the squeezed Fock-state basis, where a topologically protected scheme for preparing superpositions of squeezed Fock states is proposed. This study establishes Thouless pumping in Fock-state lattices as a useful tool for quantum state engineering, shifting the focus from observing topological transport to harnessing it for the preparation of non-classical states of the bosonic mode.

</details>


### [67] [Long-lived telecom-heralded single-photon storage in an absorptive spin-rephased quantum memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02579)
*Alberto E. Rodríguez-Moldes,Félicien Appas,Jonathan Hänni,Jelena V. Rakonjac,Samuele Grandi,Hugues de Riedmatten*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates long-lived storage of telecom-heralded single photons in a Pr$^{3+}$:Y$_2$SiO$_5$ quantum memory using a new spin rephasing technique, achieving efficient photon echoes and establishing non-classical correlations essential for quantum networks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable long-distance entanglement distribution in quantum networks by achieving long-lived storage of single photons with minimal decoherence effects.

Method: Utilizing an atomic frequency comb (AFC) spin-wave protocol combined with a XY4 spin rephasing sequence in a Pr$^{3+}$:Y$_2$SiO$_5$ quantum memory.

Result: Achieved storage times of approximately 3 ms for classical photon echoes and non-classical correlations with storage times of up to 180 μs, with cross-correlation values reaching 4.6(4).

Conclusion: These results mark a significant advancement toward scalable quantum repeater links by enabling efficient, multiplexed storage of single photons.

Abstract: Long-lived storage of single photons under the form of atomic excitations is at the foundation of long-distance entanglement distribution in quantum networks. To mitigate decoherence effects induced by the environment, rephasing of the hyperfine coherences using microwave pulses have been implemented in a variety of single-emitter and ensemble-based solid-state systems. However, the demonstration of storage of single photons in an absorptive quantum memory including such spin rephasing mechanism remains elusive. In this work, we show non-classical storage of telecom-heralded single photons in a Pr$^{3+}$:Y$_2$SiO$_5$ rare-earth ion doped crystal quantum memory using the atomic frequency comb (AFC) spin-wave protocol combined with a XY4 spin rephasing sequence. Long-lived AFC photon echoes are first observed in the classical regime for storage times of up to approximately 3 ms. We then demonstrate non-classical correlations between heralding photons and stored signal photons generated by a cavity-enhanced parametric photon-pair source for storage times of up to 180 $μ$s and with measured cross-correlation values as high as 4.6(4). Together with the capacity of Pr$^{3+}$:Y$_2$SiO$_5$ QMs to support highly efficient and multiplexed storage, this result represents a significant step towards scalable long-distance quantum repeater links.

</details>


### [68] [The advantages of extended nonreciprocal quantum batteries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02593)
*Meng-Long Song,Zan Cao,Hai-Tao Dong,Si-Yu Zhang,Xue-Ke Song,Liu Ye,Dong Wang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study examines extended nonreciprocal quantum batteries, highlighting their superior energy storage and transfer compared to traditional systems, emphasizing resonance, detuning effects, and practical implications for energy efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore advanced energy storage solutions that outperform traditional quantum battery systems in terms of efficiency and stability during energy transfer.

Method: The analysis involves studying the detuning effects between charging systems and pumps, stability of energy storage in various battery configurations, and energy distribution during charging processes.

Result: Extended nonreciprocal quantum batteries demonstrate nearly infinite storage capacity in weakly localized environments and superior energy transfer capabilities compared to reciprocal charging methods.

Conclusion: The research supports nonreciprocal charging as a more efficient and potent method for energy storage and transfer, advocating for its practical application.

Abstract: This study investigates the performance of extended nonreciprocal quantum batteries (QBs), as well as its advantages in energy storage and energy transfer compared to reciprocal charging and the original nonreciprocal batteries. After analyzing the detuning between the charging system and the external pump, we discover that resonance is a key factor in maintaining high-energy batteries and high charging power; furthermore, the detuning of the charger or battery determines the stability of the charging process for different structures. Research on steady-state energy storage in batteries revealed that single-threaded or multi-threaded charging can achieve nearly infinite energy storage in weakly localized environments, thereby demonstrating the significant energy advantages of extended nonreciprocal quantum batteries. Finally, by considering the energy distribution within the charging system, we observe that nonreciprocal charging offers energy transfer advantages unmatched by reciprocal charging; the former achieves a comprehensive balance between charging cost and energy storage capacity that the latter cannot match. As a novel and superior charging protocol, our findings are expected to provide a potent reference for the promotion and practical implementation of nonreciprocal charging.

</details>


### [69] [Heuristically optimizing, synthesizing, and prioritizing measurement settings for quantum state tomography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02633)
*Sumukh S. Moudghalya,Anton Frisk Kockum,Akshay Gaikwad*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper reformulates operator partitioning in quantum computing as a graph-coloring problem, developing an efficient framework that enhances quantum state tomography (QST) experiments by optimizing measurement settings and prioritizing data acquisition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Efficiently partitioning sets of quantum operators is crucial for applications like quantum simulation and QST, yet traditional methods are computationally expensive as problem complexity increases.

Method: The authors reformulate the operator partitioning problem as a graph-coloring problem and implement a framework that utilizes various graph-coloring algorithms, benchmarking their performance in operator partitioning.

Result: Heuristic graph-coloring methods significantly reduce required measurement settings for QST and enable scheduling that maximizes information gain, achieving optimization in minutes on a standard laptop.

Conclusion: The framework demonstrates that graph-coloring heuristics are a scalable and practical solution for optimizing measurements in noisy intermediate-scale quantum devices.

Abstract: A key task in many quantum-computing applications, e.g., quantum simulation and quantum state tomography (QST), is to partition an arbitrary set of operators into mutually commuting subsets for efficient measurements. However, brute-force approaches to this task quickly become intractable as the number and dimensionality of operators grow. Here, we reformulate operator partitioning as a graph-coloring (GC) problem and develop an efficient computational framework to solve it, balancing accuracy and efficiency. Our framework enables leveraging a range of GC algorithms, which we benchmark for operator partitioning. Then, we demonstrate their utility in optimizing QST experiments, where determining non-overlapping data acquisition settings for QST is a major challenge, and prioritizing among these settings, i.e., selecting the experiments that provide the most information. We further show how to perform these experiments by synthesizing Clifford circuits for joint measurement of commuting Pauli operators in multi-qubit systems. We validate our framework across multi-qubit (up to five qubits), multi-qutrit (up to three qutrits), and hybrid qubit-qutrit systems. Our results show that heuristic GC methods substantially reduce the number of required measurement settings for QST and enable priority-based scheduling that maximizes the information gain per experiment. The optimization converges within minutes on a student-grade laptop, providing speedups of several orders of magnitude over brute-force methods already for these relatively small quantum systems. This demonstrates the potential of GC heuristics as a scalable and practical tool for characterization of noisy intermediate-scale quantum devices. We have made the Python implementation of our GC framework to optimize and schedule QST experiments publicly available at https://github.com/ssm8015/QST_GT.git.

</details>


### [70] [Transversal Gates and Magic State Distillation in an Optimally Synthesized Spin-Qubit Shuttling Bus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02641)
*Pau Escofet,Andrii Semenov,Niall Murphy,Elena Blokhina,Carmen G. Almudéver,Sergi Abadal,Eduard Alarcón*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This work presents a multi-qubit spin-qubit shuttling bus architecture that optimizes error correction and logical operations in quantum computing, offering improved connectivity and fidelity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for a co-designed physical architecture that supports robust error correction and efficient logical computation at scale in fault-tolerant quantum computing.

Method: Proposes a shuttling bus architecture optimizing qubit layout for syndrome extraction, enabling transversal two-qubit logical gates, and introduces ancilla-sharing for multiple logical qubits in a single element, transitioning from a 1D bus to a 2D grid.

Result: The proposed architecture achieves all-to-all logical connectivity, reduces inter-qubit distance, and consistently improves logical error, alongside optimizing the layout for a magic state distillation circuit.

Conclusion: The results advocate for a principled co-design framework linking the physical, error-correction, and logical layers of quantum computation, reinforcing the viability of spin-qubit shuttling architectures for scalable fault-tolerant setups.

Abstract: Fault-tolerant quantum computing requires not only reliable logical qubit storage, but also the ability to perform high-fidelity logical operations between error-corrected qubits at scale. While much of the existing literature focuses on optimizing syndrome extraction for a single logical qubit, the co-design of physical architectures that support both robust error correction and efficient logical computation remains an open challenge. In this work, we propose a multi-qubit spin-qubit shuttling bus architecture that addresses both requirements simultaneously. The architecture optimizes the physical qubit layout for syndrome extraction and supports transversal two-qubit logical gates between an arbitrary number of logical qubits, achieving all-to-all logical connectivity through coherent spin shuttling. We further propose an ancilla-sharing scheme that encodes multiple logical qubits within a single logical element, compressing the physical footprint of the processor and improving long-range gate fidelity. Extending the architecture from a one-dimensional bus to a two-dimensional grid of shuttling tracks reduces the inter-qubit distance, yielding consistent improvements in logical error. Finally, we apply the Quantum Reverse Mapping methodology at the logical level to optimize the layout of a \textit{15-to-1} magic state distillation circuit, demonstrating how the transversal capabilities of the proposed architecture can be leveraged for universal fault-tolerant computation. Taken together, these results establish a principled co-design framework that bridges the physical, error-correction, and logical computation layers of the quantum stack, and demonstrate that spin-qubit shuttling architectures are a viable and flexible substrate for scalable fault-tolerant quantum computation.

</details>


### [71] [Quantum Meta-Complexity Is All You Need: Characterizing One-Way Puzzles via Time-Bounded Kolmogorov Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02687)
*Morteza Saberikamarposhti*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper initiates a program to analyze quantum cryptography's complexity within specific time bounds, defining a new measure and proving significant results related to quantum coding and one-way puzzles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work aims to bridge gaps in understanding the complexity of quantum cryptography compared to classical approaches, specifically by characterizing minimal search primitives in this context.

Method: The authors define a probabilistic quantum program complexity pKq^t and prove two key theorems: a quantum coding theorem relating to output descriptions from quantum samplers and an exact characterization of one-way puzzles linked to a specific time-bound complexity problem.

Result: The quantum coding theorem shows that strings produced by quantum samplers can be described efficiently, and that the existence of one-way puzzles is tied to the difficulty of a specific time-bound problem involving pKq.

Conclusion: The paper isolates the polynomial-time coding theorem as a critical conjecture, suggesting it is foundational for the broader goals of the meta-complexity program while also addressing barriers to classical methods being adapted to quantum contexts.

Abstract: We initiate the time-bounded meta-complexity program for quantum cryptography. Recent work characterizes one-way puzzles, the minimal search primitive of quantum cryptography without one-way functions, by the average-case hardness of approximating the plain, uncomputable Kolmogorov complexity over quantumly samplable distributions; the classical program of Liu and Pass, by contrast, lives at polynomial time bounds. We define a probabilistic time-bounded quantum program complexity pKq^t for classical strings and prove two unconditional theorems. First, a quantum coding theorem: any string output by a quantum polynomial-time sampler with probability delta admits a description of the information-theoretically optimal length log(1/delta) plus logarithmic terms, decodable by a quantum machine in time O(sqrt(1/delta)) times a polynomial, via amplitude amplification over the coherently executed sampler. Second, an exact characterization at subexponential time: one-way puzzles exist if and only if the gap problem for pKq at time bound 2^(n/2) poly(n) is weakly quantum-average-hard, refining the plain-complexity characterizations. We then isolate the polynomial-time coding theorem as the single load-bearing open conjecture of the program, prove that it implies the full polynomial-time characterization, analyze why the classical derandomization proof resists quantization, and formulate a relativized barrier conjecture delimiting string-valued meta-complexity at one-way puzzles. Conjectures are labeled as such throughout.

</details>


### [72] [Quantum amplitude estimation beyond power-of-two schedules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02715)
*Farrokh Labib*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a non-adaptive quantum amplitude estimation (QAE) method that reduces query complexity using an optimal geometric depth-ladder and maximum-likelihood post-processing, achieving better performance than existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the high query complexity of non-adaptive quantum amplitude estimation compared to adaptive methods.

Method: The authors replace conventional post-processing with maximum-likelihood estimation and traditional power-of-two depth ladders with a geometric ladder, resulting in a deterministic-schedule estimator.

Result: The new method achieves a total query complexity of $2.8$-$3.1/\varepsilon$ at high confidence levels, matching adaptive methods within statistical uncertainty and improving constants significantly over non-adaptive benchmarks.

Conclusion: The optimal ratio and ladder design enhance estimation efficiency and performance, allowing for noise-aware extensions and efficient scaling while approaching the theoretical limits of performance.

Abstract: Non-adaptive quantum amplitude estimation (QAE) fixes its Grover depths in advance, so every circuit can run in parallel, but it has so far needed more queries than the best adaptive methods. We show that most of this gap comes from two conventional choices: subspace-based post-processing and power-of-two depth ladders. We replace the first by the exact maximum-likelihood estimate, one matrix multiplication per batch of estimates, and the second by a geometric ladder with ratio $r \approx 1.45$. The result is a fully parallel, deterministic-schedule estimator with total query complexity $2.8$-$3.1/\varepsilon$ at 95% confidence for target errors from $3.5\times 10^{-3}$ to $10^{-6}$. This matches the average-case complexity of chebAE, the best benchmarked adaptive method, within statistical uncertainty (with the lower point estimate at every scale tested), beats its maximum-observed complexity by $1.6\times$, and needs a maximum sequential depth of only $0.21/\varepsilon$ against chebAE's $2.9/\varepsilon$. Relative to csAE, the best non-adaptive benchmark, the constants improve by 30-35% at 95% and $1.5$-$1.7\times$ at 99% confidence. The optimal ratio has a simple origin. Doubling is the fastest depth growth at which the data can still tell neighboring candidate values apart, so power-of-two ladders sit at the edge of confusion and must buy reliability with extra shots; a slightly denser ladder checks every scale redundantly. An error-probability analysis reproduces the measured failure rates and locates the optimum. The likelihood formulation extends directly to noise-aware estimation, and uniformly scaling the capped ladder covers the depth-limited regime, realizing the optimal trade-off $M N_{\mathrm{tot}} \approx (0.4$-$0.6)/\varepsilon^2$ within $\sim 1.1\times$ of the schedule's Cramér-Rao limit.

</details>


### [73] [Effective quasiparticle conserving Lindbladians in the thermodynamic limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02725)
*Lea Lenke,Kai Phillip Schmidt*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a framework for constructing effective quasiparticle-conserving Lindbladian operators in open quantum many-body systems using continuous similarity transformations, addressing challenges in the thermodynamic limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The treatment of Lindbladian operators in the thermodynamic limit remains a significant challenge in describing open quantum many-body systems.

Method: The authors develop two methods: projective continuous similarity transformations (pcst++) and deepCST, enabling systematic block diagonalization of Lindbladian operators with respect to quasiparticle number.

Result: By applying these methods to the dissipative transverse-field Ising chain, a quasiparticle-conserving Lindbladian was derived, revealing how Ising interactions renormalize decay rates of excitations and the competition between coherence and dissipation.

Conclusion: This work establishes continuous similarity transformations as effective tools for simplifying the analysis of open quantum systems in the thermodynamic limit, enabling a clearer understanding of their quasiparticle dynamics.

Abstract: Open quantum many-body systems are commonly described by Lindblad master equations, yet the treatment of Lindbladian operators in the thermodynamic limit remains a major challenge. We develop a framework for constructing effective quasiparticle-conserving Lindbladian operators directly in the thermodynamic limit. Our approach extends continuous similarity transformations to non-Hermitian open quantum systems and enables the systematic block diagonalization of Lindbladians with respect to the quasiparticle (qp) number. We formulate two complementary methods. The first, projective continuous similarity transformations (pcst++), generalizes perturbative continuous unitary transformations to Lindblad operators so that a linked-cluster expansion allows us to obtain high-order series expansions of the infinite system. The second, deepCST, extends directly evaluated enhanced perturbative continuous unitary transformations by combining the same qp-conserving generator with a perturbative truncation scheme that yields non-perturbative effective Lindbladian operators directly in thermodynamic limit. We apply pcst++ and deepCST to the dissipative transverse-field Ising chain with local dissipation. We focus on the low-Ising regime. We purify the Lindbladian by splitting each spin into two sites. A spin flip then corresponds to two qps. We derive and analyze the effective, qp-conserving Lindbladian in the sectors with zero to two qps. We show how the Ising interaction renormalizes the decay rates of elementary spin-flip excitations and provides the microscopic mechanism for a competition between coherent interactions and dissipation. Our work establishes perturbative and non-perturbative continuous similarity transformations as a versatile tool for deriving effective qp pictures of open quantum many-body systems in the thermodynamic limit.

</details>


### [74] [Spin squeezing by geometric focusing in vacuum Rabi oscillations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02738)
*Aoqin Liang,Qi Liu,Guoqing Wang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: Vacuum Rabi oscillations can induce spin squeezing via geometric focusing on the Bloch sphere, with robust results against dissipation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the generation of spin squeezing using vacuum Rabi oscillations and understand the underlying mechanisms via geometrical aspects of the Bloch sphere.

Method: Starting from a coherent spin state and resonantly coupling it to a vacuum cavity, the study focuses on the geometric properties of the Bloch sphere that lead to spin squeezing as the collective spin approaches the atomic ground state.

Result: The study finds that the optimal Wineland squeezing parameter scales as ξ_opt^2 ∝ N^{-1/3}, indicating a delicate balance between geometric focusing and vacuum fluctuations, with a squeezing timescale defined by t_s ∼ 1/(g√N).

Conclusion: The proposed method produces robust spin squeezing and is applicable in realistic scenarios, exemplified by the potential application to $^{171}$Yb.

Abstract: We show that vacuum Rabi oscillations can directly generate spin squeezing through geometric focusing on the Bloch sphere. Starting from a coherent spin state resonantly coupled to a cavity initially in the vacuum state, quantum fluctuations are focused by the curvature of the Bloch sphere as the collective spin approaches the atomic ground state, producing squeezing transverse to the direction of motion. The squeezing timescale is set by the collective Rabi frequency $t_s\sim 1/(g\sqrt{N})$. The optimal Wineland squeezing parameter scales as $ξ_{\rm opt}^2\propto N^{-1/3}$, which is an outcome of the competition between the geometric focusing effects and the cavity-field vacuum fluctuations. The squeezing remains robust against realistic dissipation. In the end, an application example of $^{171}$Yb is briefly discussed to show the feasibility of our protocol.

</details>


### [75] [Conditional validity of quantum event classifiers under collider systematics and quantum estimation uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02781)
*Roberto Fernández-Barrios,Iker Pastor-López,Asier González-Santocildes,Pablo García Bringas*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses a quantum machine-learning classifier's failure under target data shift and finite-shot randomization, proposing a robust auditing framework to ensure valid inference despite these issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the reliability of quantum machine-learning classifiers in the presence of data shifts and randomization effects, ensuring accurate performance evaluation.

Method: An information-conditional, fail-closed auditing framework is developed that provides verdicts with controlled error rates, utilizing a deployed sampling protocol on a Higgs-to-tau-tau collider benchmark.

Result: Classifier performance metrics are shown to be unstable due to template estimation inconsistencies, affecting signal-strength inference and leading to changes in thresholded target metrics.

Conclusion: The findings demonstrate that classical controls challenge perceived quantum performance, and the research does not assert any quantum advantage.

Abstract: Claims about a deployed quantum machine-learning classifier can fail when target data shift or when finite-shot quantum evaluation randomizes the model itself. We develop an information-conditional, fail-closed auditing framework that returns supported, refuted or unresolved verdicts with anytime-valid per-claim error control under a declared sampling protocol. On a Higgs-to-tau-tau collider benchmark, stable classifier metrics do not guarantee valid signal-strength inference: at the studied finite-template statistics, the fixed-template profile can lose coverage, even in shift-free controls, when its templates are estimated independently and template-statistical uncertainty is not modeled explicitly. Across 30 frozen finite-shot quantum-kernel deployments, every realized Gram matrix is propagated through refitting, calibration and threshold selection; in the primary raw pipeline these perturbations leave ranking nearly unchanged yet move thresholded target metrics by about 0.02, flipping ideal-anchored claims, and the diagonal-loading sensitivity decomposes differently. Matched classical controls remove apparent quantum-specific nominal-performance and sensing effects. We claim no quantum advantage.

</details>


### [76] [Phonon-limited detection thresholds for genetically encoded fluorescent-protein spin-qubit relaxometry of neural radicals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02792)
*Parul Raghuvanshi,Sagnik Ganguly,Sharika E,Mohana Priya T.,Vishvendra S. Poonia*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores using a genetically encoded yellow fluorescent protein spin qubit for quantum sensing, deriving a theory to improve sensitivity to neural signaling radicals but finds significant limitations in current capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance quantum sensing at molecular scales using genetically encoded spin qubits in fluorescent proteins.

Method: Development of a detection-limit theory, derivation of a relaxation matrix, and validation against simulations and benchmarks for improved sensitivity of fluorescent-protein spin qubits.

Result: Current sensitivity is six to eight orders of magnitude below physiological levels; room-temperature relaxation is dominated by Raman processes and phonon limitations.

Conclusion: Design rules for enhancing spin qubit performance and a proposed protocol for chemical specificity are presented, addressing key bottlenecks in photon yield and relaxation times.

Abstract: The demonstration that enhanced yellow fluorescent protein hosts an optically addressable spin-1 qubit in its metastable triplet state raises the prospect of genetically encoded quantum sensing at molecular length scales. We develop a detection-limit theory for using this fluorescent-protein spin qubit (FPSQ) to sense paramagnetic neural signaling radicals by spin relaxometry. We derive the transition-resolved Redfield relaxation matrix of the zero-field-split triplet coupled to a diffusing radical bath, establish the regime in which it collapses to a single exponential, and validate it against Lindblad simulations and nitrogen-vacancy benchmarks. Propagating the effects of photon shot noise, photobleaching-grounded photon budget, and finite measurement bandwidth, we find that the native room-temperature sensor falls short of physiological sensitivity by six to eight orders of magnitude with the bottleneck being the phonon-limited intrinsic $\Tone$. Analyzing the underlying direct and two-phonon Raman processes, we show that room-temperature relaxation is Raman-dominated by $\sim\!720\!:\!1$ and that, because the Raman coefficient scales as $v^{-10}$ with sound velocity, a $\sim\!2\times$ stiffening of the chromophore environment recovers $\Tone\sim\SI{100}{\micro\second}$, sufficient for micromolar sensing. Nanomolar sensing is obstructed by a direct-process ceiling of \SI{79}{\micro\second} that vibronic decoupling alone cannot breach. We obtain quantitative design rules, identify photon yield as a co-equal bottleneck, and propose a frequency-resolved protocol for chemical specificity.

</details>


### [77] [Variational preparation of thermofield double states for SYK models via multi-angle QAOA: sequential angle pruning for circuit reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02793)
*Haji Muhammad Husnain Ashfaq,Moongul Byun,Keun-Young Kim*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a method to efficiently prepare thermofield double states using a reduced quantum circuit approach, maintaining high fidelity through sequential angle pruning in quantum approximate optimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to reduce the depth of quantum circuits for TFD-state preparation while preserving fidelity is critical for implementation on noisy quantum processors.

Method: The multi-angle quantum approximate optimization algorithm (ma-QAOA) is utilized, along with two top-down sequential angle-pruning algorithms that optimize and remove small-angle Pauli-string evolutions iteratively.

Result: The application of ma-QAOA allows for high-fidelity TFD state preparation, and the angle pruning maintains fidelity while significantly reducing circuit depth, achieving up to 92.1% removal of nonlocal evolutions with about 95% fidelity in specific models.

Conclusion: The proposed approach demonstrates a viable way to enhance TFD-state preparation in quantum processors, with potential extensions for quantum-classical hybrid implementations.

Abstract: Variational preparation of thermofield double (TFD) states can require deep quantum circuits, particularly for interacting many-body systems. Reducing these circuits while retaining high fidelity is therefore crucial for TFD-state preparation on noisy quantum processors. We study this problem by applying the multi-angle quantum approximate optimization algorithm (ma-QAOA) to TFD-state preparation and introducing two top-down sequential angle-pruning algorithms. Starting from the optimized initial ma-QAOA circuit, both algorithms sequentially remove Pauli-string evolutions with small optimized angles and reoptimize the remaining parameters after each removal. We apply these algorithms to Gaussian and binary Sachdev--Ye--Kitaev (SYK) models in both dense and sparse cases. We find that ma-QAOA prepares the target TFD states with high fidelity and that sequential small-angle pruning retains high fidelity while reducing the circuit depth, particularly at low temperature. Moreover, using the post-reoptimization cost in sequential small-angle pruning further improves the fidelity. For the binary sparse $N=10$ SYK model at $β=10$, $88.8\%$--$92.1\%$ of the nonlocal Pauli-string evolutions are removed while retaining an average fidelity of approximately $95\%$. Finally, we propose extensions of the sequential pruning algorithms toward quantum--classical hybrid implementation.

</details>


### [78] [Model-level synthetic-flux control of hyperchaos order and matched-resource sensing in dissipative optomechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02827)
*Stella Rolande Mbokop Tchounda,Carolle Tchodimou,Philippe Djorwe,Sifeu Takougang Kingni,Serge Guy Nana Engo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the impact of synthetic flux in a six-dimensional model of dissipative optomechanics, focusing on hyperchaos transitions and their effects on force sensing, finding no enhancement on the chaotic attractor despite significant theoretical developments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to explore the chaotic dynamics in a complex optomechanical system and assess the relevance of synthetic flux in enhancing performance, particularly in force sensing applications.

Method: A six-dimensional model is employed, utilizing a phase-consistent Floquet–Lyapunov protocol and various mathematical checks, including monodromy multipliers and Monte Carlo simulations, to map hyperchaos transitions and evaluate sensing capabilities.

Result: The synthetic flux influences the order of transition to hyperchaos, generating up to four unstable Lyapunov directions, but does not improve sensing capabilities on the chaotic attractor, with specific Fisher gain metrics reported.

Conclusion: Results indicate that while the model offers novel insights into hyperchaos and stability, its findings are confined to theoretical explorations, necessitating further experimental validation to align with practical silicon optomechanical systems.

Abstract: Within a normalised six-dimensional model of dissipative optomechanics (one cavity + two mechanical resonators), a synthetic-flux phase $Φ_{\rm syn}$ selects the \emph{order} of a drive- and coupling-gated hyperchaos transition---up to four simultaneously unstable Lyapunov directions, beyond any reported single-mode benchmark---while the same matched-resource force-sensing protocol yields no flux-induced enhancement on the chaotic attractor. Building on the topology of Muthukumar \emph{et al.}~[PR Applied \textbf{24}, 014053 (2025)], a phase-consistent Floquet--Lyapunov protocol (cross-checked by monodromy multipliers, dissipative volume balance and a 180-run three-seed audit) localises a Neimark--Sacker bifurcation at $E^{*}=\num{1.060}$ ($θ=0$). At a weakly coupled reference the matched Fisher gain reaches at most $\num{1.32}\times$ (flux-off) and $\num{1.16}\times$ (single-mode), with Monte-Carlo median $\num{1.039}\times$ (90\,\% CI $[\num{1.025},\num{1.053}]$); on the chaotic attractor the identical protocol returns a null result ($\mathcal{G}_{A/B}=\num{1.039}\pm\num{0.014}$). Truncated-Fock and truncated-Wigner checks support the mean-field description at selected points. Both the hyperchaos classification and the sensing result remain strictly model-level: the strong-coupling sector explored here lies $\num{2542}\times$ beyond anchored silicon optomechanical couplings. Closing that gap requires a measured inter-resonator hopping $J_m$ and fixed bath temperatures.

</details>


### [79] [Exponential speedup of polarization stabilization for long distance DWDM quantum networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02841)
*Jinyi Du,En Teng Lim,Xingjian Zhang,Hongwei Gao,George F. R. Chen,Dawn T. H. Tan,Alexander Ling*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study presents a method for stabilizing polarization in fibre-based quantum networks using entangled-photon feedback to avoid noise and calibration downtime, demonstrated over a 133 km testbed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Stable transmission of polarization entanglement is crucial for reliable quantum networks, but traditional feedback methods introduce noise or downtime.

Method: The authors combine wavelength-bracketed probing and switch-enabled path decomposition to use entangled-photon sidebands for tracking polarization without interrupting the main channel.

Result: The method was demonstrated on a 133 km fibre testbed, achieving continuous stabilization for over 24 hours without classical light interference.

Conclusion: The approach offers a scalable strategy for stabilizing wavelength-multiplexed quantum networks by breaking down multi-link measurements into single-link evaluations, minimizing downtime.

Abstract: Fibre-based quantum networks distributing polarization entanglement require a stable and uninterrupted transmission basis for reliable operation. Bright classical reference light enables rapid polarization feedback but can introduce noise into quantum channels. Entangled-photon-based feedback avoids this noise, but typically interrupts the target entanglement channel during calibration and becomes prohibitively slow over long distances due to the product loss of fibre links. Here, we overcome both limitations by combining wavelength-bracketed probing with switch-enabled path decomposition. Spectrally adjacent entangled-photon sidebands track the polarization response of the central distribution channel without interrupting its transmission, while optical switches and local reference fibres independently determine the signal and idler network transformations. Transferring the resulting compensation settings to the central channel eliminates calibration-induced downtime and changes the acquisition-time scaling from the product of the link losses to the sum of losses. We demonstrate the method on a 133 km fibre testbed and achieve continuous closed-loop stabilization for more than 24 hours without classical reference light. The decomposition of multi-link quantum feedback into single-link measurements provides a scalable stabilization strategy for wavelength-multiplexed quantum networks.

</details>


### [80] [A quantum oracle separation between QMA(2) and QMA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02865)
*John Bostanci,Sabee Grewal,Jonas Haferkamp,Andrew Huang,Yeongwoo Hwang,Anand Natarajan,Chinmay Nirkhe*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: A quantum oracle shows $	ext{QMA} 
eq 	ext{QMA}(2)$, resolving the no-disentanglers conjecture requiring exponential input size for $(	ext{ε},	ext{δ})$-disentanglers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the relationship between quantum complexity classes $	ext{QMA}$ and $	ext{QMA}(2)$ and to resolve the long-standing no-disentanglers conjecture.

Method: The proof employs She and Yuen's unitarily invariant polynomial method, alongside a new approach involving symmetric and antisymmetric subspace projectors to analyze the $	ext{QMA}$ lower bound.

Result: Identified a quantum oracle that differentiates between $	ext{QMA}$ and $	ext{QMA}(2)$, demonstrating the requirement of exponential input sizes for certain disentanglers.

Conclusion: The no-disentanglers conjecture is resolved, establishing foundational limits on the efficiency of $(ε,δ)$-disentanglers in quantum computing.

Abstract: We find a quantum oracle relative to which $\mathsf{QMA} \neq \mathsf{QMA}(2)$. As a consequence, we resolve the no-disentanglers conjecture of Watrous: for every $ε+δ<1$, any $(ε,δ)$-disentangler requires input size exponential in the number of output qubits. Our proof combines the unitarily invariant polynomial method of She and Yuen (ITCS '23) with a new construction based on the symmetric and antisymmetric subspace projectors, reducing the $\mathsf{QMA}$ lower bound to the approximate degree of $\mathrm{OR}$.

</details>

### [81] [Radiation Hardness of Commercially Available NUV-MT Silicon Photomultipliers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01809)
*T. Avgitas,M. Axiotis,Z. Balmforth,I. Manthos,K. Nikolopoulos,E. Taimpiri,C. Toukmenidis,A. Ziagkova*

Main category: physics.ins-det

TL;DR: NUV-MT SiPMs show promise for particle physics applications but degrade significantly under neutron irradiation, with thermal annealing offering partial recovery of performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the behavior of NUV-MT SiPMs in high-radiation environments and ensure reliability for particle-physics experiments.

Method: Commercial NUV-MT SiPMs were irradiated with neutrons and characterized pre- and post-irradiation, including thermal-annealing stages.

Result: Irradiation increased dark noise significantly and degraded photon resolution, with complete loss at high fluence; thermal annealing mitigated some damage.

Conclusion: Thermal annealing can restore some performance metrics of irradiated SiPMs, making them more viable for applications in high-radiation settings.

Abstract: Silicon Photomultipliers (SiPMs) based on the near-ultraviolet, metal-filled trench (NUV-MT) technology offer improved photon detection efficiency and reduced correlated noise relative to earlier designs, making them attractive for a broad range of particle- and astroparticle-physics applications. As such devices may be deployed in high-radiation environments, quantifying their performance after irradiation is essential. Commercially available Broadcom AFBR-S4N series NUV-MT SiPMs were irradiated with 1 MeV neutrons to fluences between 2x10^{9} and 1.1x10^{10} n_{eq}/cm^2 at the TANDEM accelerator facility of NCSR "Demokritos" in Athens, and characterised before and after irradiation and over successive thermal-annealing stages. Irradiation increased the dark noise by up to three orders of magnitude and degraded the single-photon resolution by up to a factor of ten, with resolution lost entirely at the highest fluence, while no shift in breakdown voltage was observed. Thermal annealing partially recovered the performance, reducing the dark noise by up to a factor of two and restoring the single-photon resolution.

</details>


### [82] [Characterization of a low-power 3D photon-to-digital converter readout improved for system integration in meter-scale applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01841)
*Olivier Lepage,Tommy Rossignol,Nicolas Roy,Gabriel Lessard,Frédéric Vachon,Lorenzo Fabris,Serge A. Charlebois,Jean-François Pratte*

Main category: physics.ins-det

TL;DR: The paper details an enhanced readout integrated circuit (ROIC) for digital silicon photomultipliers (dSiPM), achieving improved timing jitter, adjustable dead time, and reduced power consumption, ready for 3D integration in photon-to-digital converters (PDCs) for various applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the performance and efficiency of digital silicon photomultipliers (dSiPM) by developing a more effective readout integrated circuit (ROIC) that allows for better detection capabilities in various scientific and medical fields.

Method: The study presents a 3D-integrated ROIC designed for a 64 x 64 pixel architecture, fabricated in TSMC 180 nm technology, with features like a flag output for pixel triggering information and a tunable hold-off circuit for managing afterpulsing and variations.

Result: The improved ROIC reduces timing jitter from 72.0 ps RMS to 22.6 ps RMS, adjustable dead time between 32 ns and 18 μs, and lowers power consumption by 30% at trigger rates over 10 kHz, validating its readiness for integration into PDCs.

Conclusion: The advancements in the ROIC make it suitable for integration in photon-to-digital converters, enabling applications in medical imaging, particle physics, and quantum sciences.

Abstract: Digital silicon photomultipliers (dSiPM) are arrays of single photon avalanche diodes (SPADs) where each SPAD has its own electronic readout. To maximize the photodetection area, we developed a readout integrated circuit (ROIC) 3D-integrated to a custom-designed SPAD layer fabricated at Teledyne Dalsa (Bromont, Canada) to form a photon-to-digital converter (PDC). This paper presents an improved version of a previously demonstrated ROIC, fabricated in TSMC 180 nm technology to manage a 64 x 64 pixel architecture. This ROIC has different outputs, such as a flag output that produces a pulse whenever one of the pixels triggers and a digital sum that samples the amount of triggered SPADs. Improvements lead to a reduction of the timing jitter on the flag output from 72.0 ps RMS to 22.6 ps RMS through optimized H-tree design. A tunable hold-off circuit provides adjustable dead time from 32 ns to 18 μs, addressing both afterpulsing mitigation and SPAD-to-SPAD variations due to process defects. Power consumption is lowered by 30% at trigger rates exceeding 10 kHz through digital logic optimization and clock gating. These measurements validated that the ROIC is ready for 3D integration into a PDC for systems in medical imaging, particle physics, and quantum sciences.

</details>


### [83] [Toward High-Resolution Detection of Target Fragmentation: TEA-Sensitized NIT for Proton Therapy Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02337)
*V. Boccia,T. Asada,the FOOT collaboration*

Main category: physics.ins-det

TL;DR: This paper presents enhanced sensitivity for Nano Imaging Trackers (NIT) in detecting target fragmentation in proton therapy, using new sensitization methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the tracking efficiency of primary and secondary protons in proton therapy measurement applications, particularly in light of limitations observed in previous measurements.

Method: Alternative chemical sensitization methods (Au-S and TEA) were employed, coupled with the GR-1 developer, to enhance the sensitivity of NIT.

Result: The new sensitization methods improved NIT's detection capabilities across various types of particle exposures, including relevant clinical proton energies.

Conclusion: Enhanced NIT sensitivity allows for better tracking of charged particles in proton therapy, potentially improving the efficiency of target-fragmentation measurements.

Abstract: Nano Imaging Trackers (NIT) are fine-grained nuclear emulsions capable of tracking charged particles with sub-micrometric spatial resolution. The DAMON (Direct Measurement of Target Fragmentation) experiment recently employed NIT to detect target-fragmentation events relevant to proton therapy in direct kinematics. These measurements showed that the small crystal size, while providing high spatial resolution, limits the reconstruction efficiency for primary proton tracks and high-energy secondary protons. This work reports an enhancement of NIT sensitivity achieved through alternative chemical sensitization methods, namely gold-plus-sulfur (Au-S) and triethanolamine (TEA), together with the use of the GR-1 developer. The detector response was evaluated with gamma-ray, carbon-ion and proton exposures, including clinically relevant proton energies.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [84] [Axion Quality from Exact Proton Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01712)
*Joe Davighi,Admir Greljo,Xavier Ponce Díaz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A discrete $	ext{Z}_9$ gauge symmetry effectively prevents proton decay and can lead to viable models involving dark matter and leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a gauge symmetry that both protects against proton decay and provides a framework for neutrino masses, while also addressing dark matter and heavy relics.

Method: The paper constructs models based on a discrete $	ext{Z}_9$ gauge symmetry resulting from Higgsing a $U(1)_X$, incorporating a chiral heavy-quark sector for axion dynamics, and analyzes consequences under different inflation scenarios and PQ breaking.

Result: The models indicate successful prevention of proton decay, predict the existence of a light Goldstone, and allow for viable thermal leptogenesis and dark matter scenarios, subject to certain dimension constraints on PQ-violating operators.

Conclusion: Regions of parameter space have been identified where high-quality axions can constitute dark matter, ensuring proton stability and compatibility with leptogenesis.

Abstract: A discrete, anomaly-free $\mathbb Z_9$ gauge symmetry may persist to the deep infrared, exactly forbidding proton decay. This $\mathbb Z_9$ can emerge from Higgsing a lepton-flavour non-universal $U(1)_X$ that generates realistic neutrino masses and mixings through a high-scale seesaw and predicts an additional light Goldstone. Adding an anomaly-free chiral heavy-quark sector turns this mode into the QCD axion. The same selection rules that ban proton decay also forbid PQ-violating operators below a suitably high dimension, $d_{\rm PQ}\gtrsim10$, including operators involving the heavy quarks that radiatively match onto the scalar potential. We construct explicit models viable when PQ breaking occurs before or after inflation; in the latter case, avoiding stable heavy relics and eliminating the string-wall network tightly constrain the heavy-quark spectrum. We identify restricted regions of parameter space where thermal leptogenesis remains viable and, for $d_{\rm PQ}\geq13$, can coexist with a high-quality axion constituting all of the dark matter.

</details>


### [85] [SN1987A constraints on the neutrino-dark-fermion interaction from resonant scattering with C$ν$B](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01716)
*Christina Gao,Ke Hu,Kun-Feng Lyu,Vincent Zou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores constraints on neutrino-scalar coupling using SN1987A data due to neutrino self-interactions with light scalars, which can enhance dark radiation effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address cosmological tensions and investigate potential mechanisms for neutrino mass generation, particularly the role of light scalars in neutrino interactions.

Method: Analysis of SN1987A data through Bayesian methods, examining two models of supernova neutrino emission and different coupling types.

Result: Constraints derived on the neutrino-scalar coupling indicate that new-physics limits are dependent on various factors including heavy-flavor emission and coupling structure.

Conclusion: Future observations from Hyper-Kamiokande may provide significant upper bounds on neutrino-scalar coupling, even considering flavor conversion effects.

Abstract: Neutrino self-interactions mediated by a light scalar offer a compelling resolution to cosmological tensions and may naturally arise in neutrino mass generation mechanisms. When the scalar also couples to a light dark-sector fermion, supernova neutrinos can resonantly annihilate with the cosmic neutrino background (C$ν$B) into invisible dark radiation, depleting the flux en route to Earth. We use this depletion to constrain the neutrino-scalar coupling from the SN1987A data. Within a Bayesian framework we analyze the data for two supernova neutrino emission models, a parameterized model and a 2D hydrodynamic simulation, and for two coupling types, a mass-independent one and a mass-proportional one. Our results show that new-physics limits from SN1987A cannot be quoted independently of the heavy-flavor emission, the flavor conversion, or the coupling structure. Finally, we forecast that a future high-statistics burst recorded by Hyper-Kamiokande would restore a meaningful upper bound even when flavor conversion is included.

</details>


### [86] [Primordial Black Hole Assisted Dirac Leptogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01717)
*Marco Manno,Anish Ghoshal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Dirac leptogenesis is enhanced by evaporating primordial black holes (PBHs), enabling exploration of baryon asymmetry across a broader mass range of charged-scalar mediators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how primordial black holes impact the mechanism of Dirac leptogenesis and baryon asymmetry generation.

Method: A coupled Boltzmann system is solved considering thermal production, Hawking emission of mediators and right-handed neutrinos, and PBH contributions to expansion rate and entropy.

Result: Hawking emission from PBHs can effectively populate the charged-scalar mediator, enhancing baryon asymmetry, especially in regions where thermal production is weak.

Conclusion: PBHs extend the viable mass range for mediators, affect CP asymmetries, and contribute to energy in the right-handed neutrino sector, impacting $ΔN_{\text{eff}}$ measurements.

Abstract: Dirac leptogenesis offers a qualitatively different route to the baryon asymmetry: total lepton number is conserved, but equal and opposite asymmetries are stored in the Standard Model and right handed neutrino sectors, with only the visible component processed by electroweak sphalerons. We present the first dedicated study of how evaporating primordial black holes (PBHs) modify this mechanism. In a minimal charged-scalar mediator setup, we solve the coupled Boltzmann system including thermal production, Hawking emission of the mediator $X$ and of right handed neutrinos, the PBH contribution to the expansion rate, and entropy injection. We find that Hawking emission can populate $X$ in regions where thermal production is inefficient, allowing its subsequent CP-violating decays to enhance the baryon asymmetry. For representative benchmarks, PBHs extend the successful region from mediator masses around $10^9\,{\rm GeV}$ down to about $10^6\,{\rm GeV}$ and can compensate for smaller effective CP asymmetries. This effect is not universal: where thermal Dirac leptogenesis is already efficient, PBHs can instead reduce the final asymmetry. Their evaporation also deposits energy in the right handed neutrino sector and contributes to $ΔN_{\rm eff}$. In the PBH parameter scan, part of the region that reproduces the observed baryon asymmetry lies below the Planck reference level $ΔN_{\rm eff}=0.284$ and above the projected CMB-S4 sensitivity $ΔN_{\rm eff}=0.06$.

</details>


### [87] [Electromagnetic tails of $B$-meson light-cone distribution amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01718)
*Max Ferré,Matthias Neubert*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores electromagnetic corrections to the B-meson decay constant and light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs), revealing new insights into negative momentum values and their implications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend understanding of B-meson properties under the influence of electromagnetic corrections and their impact on LCDAs within heavy-quark effective theory.

Method: Utilizing heavy-quark effective theory, the study derives model-independent expressions for QED-generalized LCDAs and examines their asymptotic behavior via integrals over two- and three-particle LCDAs from pure QCD.

Result: Findings yield consistent results for QED-generalized LCDAs, particularly $Φ_+$, in alignment with the renormalization-group equations and the study of their evolution through one-loop anomalous dimensions.

Conclusion: A proposed phenomenological model aligns with the derived constraints, enhancing the understanding of B-meson dynamics in quantum electrodynamics.

Abstract: We investigate electromagnetic corrections to the $B$-meson decay constant and light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) within the framework of heavy-quark effective theory. The inclusion of QED effects extends the support of the LCDAs to negative values of the light-cone momentum. We clarify the relation between this region and QED-generalized decay constants sensitive to electromagnetic final-state radiation. We derive model-independent expressions for the asymptotic behavior in the large negative-momentum region of the QED-generalized LCDAs $Φ_\pm(ω,μ)$ in terms of integrals over the two- and three-particle LCDAs defined in pure QCD. We show that our result for $Φ_+$ is consistent with the renormalization-group equation satisfied by this object and study its evolution using the known one-loop anomalous dimension. Finally, we propose a phenomenological model consistent with these constraints

</details>


### [88] [Tremaine-Gunn Control: Evading Bounds on Light Fermion Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01727)
*Joel Barir,Diego Blas,Anubhav Mathur,Tomer Volansky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Dark-sector interactions can weaken the Pauli exclusion principle's restrictions on fermionic dark matter mass, allowing eV-scale fermions to form bound structures resembling dwarf galaxies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the traditional view that the Pauli exclusion principle imposes a strict lower mass limit on fermionic dark matter in galaxies.

Method: The authors derive the dynamical behavior of a dark-matter fluid influenced by an attractive finite-range scalar Yukawa force that balances degeneracy pressure in dwarf-galaxy-sized structures.

Result: The model shows that eV-scale fermions can exist in stable bound structures by circumventing conventional mass constraints, leading to new possibilities for light fermionic dark matter.

Conclusion: Dark-sector interactions significantly alter conventional phase-space limits, offering a new paradigm for understanding the formation of light fermionic dark matter.

Abstract: Pauli exclusion is often regarded as imposing a model-independent lower bound on the mass of fermionic dark matter in galaxies. We show that dark-sector interactions can substantially weaken this conclusion. In particular, eV-scale fermions can form bound structures with the characteristic sizes and densities of dwarf galaxies, thereby circumventing the conventional Tremaine-Gunn mass constraint. Within these objects, the degeneracy pressure is balanced by an attractive finite-range scalar Yukawa force that is significantly stronger than gravity. The interaction acts on dwarf-galaxy scales while remaining screened on larger scales. We derive the evolution of the resulting dark-matter fluid and identify a viable cosmological history in which structures first form gravitationally, with the new interaction becoming active only at late times. As a proof of concept, we construct a model in which a single late-time phase transition generates the required dynamics. These results provide a new route to exceptionally light fermionic dark matter and demonstrate that dark-sector interactions can qualitatively alter conventional phase-space limits.

</details>


### [89] [A PQ-Symmetric High-Scale SUSY Interpretation of the LZ High-Energy Recoil](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01892)
*Wen Yin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper interprets a 248-keV event in LZ as a sign of Higgsino dark matter and proposes a theory involving supersymmetry and a Peccei–Quinn sector to address various cosmic and particle physics issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain a significant nuclear-recoil-like event in LZ data as potentially arising from dark matter interactions, while also addressing theoretical inconsistencies in supersymmetry and related phenomena.

Method: The paper analyzes the implications of a specific Higgsino dark matter mass spectrum and its interaction properties, taking into account the effects of a neutral-Higgsino mass splitting and supersymmetry.

Result: A compatible dark matter model is proposed that includes a Higgsino mass spectrum and predicts a lower GUT scale with suppressed proton decay due to a high supersymmetry scale.

Conclusion: The combination of Higgsino and axion dark matter could alleviate constraints from current experiments, suggesting a viable framework for understanding dark matter and associated particle physics challenges.

Abstract: The 248-keV nuclear-recoil-like event reported by LZ can be interpreted as near-threshold upscattering of 1.08-TeV thermal Higgsino dark matter. A neutral-Higgsino splitting of order $0.3$--$0.35$ MeV places the fixed weak-interaction cross section close to the observed LZ interval and implies electroweak gauginos at ${O}(10^7)$ GeV. This spectrum is compatible with gravity-mediated high-scale supersymmetry and a Kim--Nilles Peccei--Quinn sector. Although the GUT scale is predicted to be lower than the conventional one, proton decay is suppressed due to the high SUSY scale which also solves the conventional CP, flavor, gravitino and moduli problems. Indeed, proton decay may be probed in the future. The PQ symmetry lowers the Higgsino mass below the SUSY scale and also yields a QCD axion that solves the strong-$CP$ problem. If the axion and Higgsino constitute mixed dark matter, the halo-profile-dependent H.E.S.S. limit can be relaxed.

</details>


### [90] [Recoil Geometry Unmasks Gluon Saturation in Forward $Z^0$ Production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02356)
*Wanchen Li,Ding Yu Shao,Shu-Yi Wei,Jian Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper shows how fiducial recoil subtraction enhances detection of gluon saturation effects in forward $Z^0$ production by isolating transverse-momentum broadening, revealing insights into small-$x$ nuclear dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding gluon saturation's effects on transverse momentum in high-energy nuclear collisions is essential for exploring quantum chromodynamics (QCD), but radiation effects often obscure these signals.

Method: The study employs fiducial recoil subtraction to create a 'transverse-momentum projector,' isolating residual momentum from recoil measured in a specific rapidity range, and combines this with a Color Glass Condensate (CGC) framework and soft-collinear effective theory (SCET) for resummation analysis.

Result: The analysis shows that the effective hard scale is reduced significantly when a benchmark rapidity range is considered, allowing for observation of gluon saturation effects under specific conditions, revealing clearer proton-nucleus separation in observables.

Conclusion: Detector geometry can be strategically employed to control perturbative recoil, providing a means to probe nonlinear QCD dynamics in small-$x$ regimes.

Abstract: Gluon saturation produces characteristic transverse-momentum broadening in nuclei, but QCD radiation largely washes out this signature. We show that fiducial recoil subtraction turns detector acceptance into a transverse-momentum projector that unmasks the broadening in forward $Z^0$ production. Subtracting the hadronic recoil measured in a chosen rapidity interval from the boson transverse momentum defines a residual momentum. At leading power, the radiative recoil in this interval cancels, while the residual momentum retains sensitivity to the small-$x$ nuclear field. Combining a CGC description of the small-$x$ target with soft-collinear effective theory (SCET) resummation for finite rapidity coverage, we find that a benchmark rapidity coverage $|η^{\rm lab}|<2.5$ lowers the effective hard scale from $M_Z\simeq 91.2$ GeV to about $7.5~\mathrm{GeV}$ of the Sudakov evolution. Increasing the saturation scale broadens the residual-momentum distribution and weakens recoil alignment, whereas wider coverage makes the proton--nucleus separation clearer in both observables. Detector geometry thus provides tunable control over perturbative recoil, enabling a probe of nonlinear small-$x$ QCD.

</details>


### [91] [Generalized Foldy-Wouthuysen approach for the derivation of non-relativistic effective field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01980)
*Tobias Asano,Fabio Di Pumpo,Enno Giese,Motoaki Bamba*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a method for deriving non-relativistic effective field theories (EFTs) from relativistic EFTs using a generalized NR reduction, establishing hidden Lorentz invariance and its applications in non-inertial spacetimes and beyond the Standard Model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for deriving effective field theories in non-relativistic regimes while maintaining the underlying symmetry structures, especially when extending to more complex and non-standard models.

Method: Constructing relativistic EFTs followed by a generalized non-relativistic reduction using an extended Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation.

Result: Successfully derived a quantum chromo-electrodynamics EFT for inertial flat spacetime, confirming the established Lagrangian of non-relativistic quantum chromodynamics and electrodynamics, with hidden Lorentz invariance emerging naturally.

Conclusion: The proposed method not only preserves the hidden Lorentz invariance but also offers a framework for deriving NR limits of theories in non-inertial spacetimes and extensions beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: Effective field theories (EFTs) are a powerful framework for performing high-precision calculations at reduced complexity compared to their fundamental counterparts. A particularly important class of EFTs arises in the non-relativistic (NR) regime. Their construction relies on a different realization of the underlying symmetries, since Lorentz invariance is no longer manifest in covariant form in the NR regime. This behavior imposes a link between certain matching coefficients, and therefore additional constraints, commonly referred to as hidden Lorentz invariance. These constraints are established in quantum field theories on inertial flat spacetime, such as NR quantum electrodynamics. However, deriving these constraints becomes considerably more involved for theories involving physics beyond the Standard Model or formulated in non-inertial spacetime backgrounds, where the hidden symmetry structure is less transparent. In this work, we present an approach to obtain the NR EFT by first constructing a relativistic EFT and then performing a generalized NR reduction based on an extended Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation. We illustrate this method by a quantum chromo-electrodynamics EFT for inertial flat spacetime, describing both electromagnetic and strong interactions, and show how it reduces to the established Lagrangian of NR quantum chromodynamics and electrodynamics. The hidden Lorentz invariance emerges as a direct consequence of the construction. This approach provides a route to obtain the NR limits of more complex theories, \eg Dirac fields in non-inertial spacetime or extensions involving physics beyond the Standard Model. As an example, we apply the method to add the coupling of a pseudoscalar axion field in a simplified model and derive its NR limit.

</details>


### [92] [Strong Constraints on Higgsino Dark Matter from Solar Capture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02775)
*Maxim Pospelov,Harikrishnan Ramani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Higgsino dark matter's behavior, particularly in a quasi-Dirac regime, and evaluates its neutrino flux from annihilation processes against IceCube's observational limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate Higgsino dark matter as a viable candidate and assess its interaction implications through neutrino detection.

Method: The study calculates the expected neutrino flux resulting from Higgsino dark matter annihilation ($χχ	o W^+W^-,ZZ$) in the Sun, accounting for varying mass splitting ($δ$) and using observational data from the IceCube experiment.

Result: The analysis provides a robust limit for the mass splitting $δ > 566$ keV, based on the absence of corresponding high-energy neutrinos detected from the Sun.

Conclusion: The findings exclude certain interpretations of an observed LZ event in terms of Higgsino dark matter via endothermic inelastic scattering with xenon nuclei.

Abstract: Higgsino dark matter remains one of the most convincing dark matter candidates. It can exist in a ``quasi-Dirac" regime, when the splitting between two Majorana components is on the order of $O(\rm few~\,MeV)$ or less. Owing to the strong gravitational pull of the Sun, the DM particles accelerate to up to $\sim 1400$\,km/sec in the solar core, which allows for inelastic scattering on heavy elements and very effective capture onto solar-bound trajectories. We evaluate the expected flux of the neutrinos from the expected $χχ\to W^+W^-,ZZ$ annihilation for the cosmologically preferred mass, $m_χ\simeq 1.08$\,TeV while keeping the mass splitting $δ$ as a free parameter. Confronting it with the non-observation of high-energy neutrinos from the Sun by the IceCube experiment, we obtain a robust limit, $δ>566$\,keV. These limits exclude the interpretation of the recent LZ event in terms of endothermic inelastic scattering of Higgsino dark matter on xenon nuclei.

</details>


### [93] [Extension of the non-pole technique to the twist-3 gluon distribution contribution in $pp$ collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02130)
*Longjie Chen,Shinsuke Yoshida*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper addresses the Sivers effect and its complexities in hard parton scattering, proposing a nonpole method to calculate it consistently across various processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the discrepancies in applying the nonpole formalism from the Collins effect to the Sivers effect, particularly in $pp$ collisions.

Method: Development of a nonpole calculation method that can be applied to the Sivers effect in any collision process.

Result: The method successfully reconciles the calculation of the Sivers effect in $ep$ and $pp$ collisions, addressing complications due to initial and final-state interactions.

Conclusion: The new nonpole method enables comprehensive analysis of the Sivers effect across different types of collisions.

Abstract: It is known that the Sivers effect is described as a twist-3 effect within the collinear factorization framework and has the characteristic feature that it arises from the pole contribution in a hard parton scattering. The calculation formalism that focuses only on the pole part was established in the 2000s and SSAs were calculated for many processes in $ep$ and $pp$ collisions. On the other hand, there also exists a contribution from twist-3 fragmentation functions regarded as the Collins effect. The Collins effect arises from the nonpole part of the hard scattering and its calculation is formulated in a somewhat different manner from that for the Sivers effect. In recent years, some attempts have been made to revisit the Sivers effect by applying the ``nonpole'' formalism developed for the Collins effect. For $ep$ collisions, this approach has successfully reproduced the known results. For $pp$ collisions, however, the presence of both initial-state-interaction and final-state-interaction makes the calculation more complicated and the known results have not yet been reproduced. In this paper, we provide a solution to this problem and develop the nonpole method so that it can be applied to the Sivers effect in any process.

</details>


### [94] [Radiative and Dalitz decays of $Υ(1S)$ in the light of the ATOMKI X17 anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02874)
*Chien-Thang Tran,Mikhail A. Ivanov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines the decay processes of Υ(1S) using the Covariant Confined Quark Model, providing predictions for various decay constants and branching fractions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze the decay processes of Υ(1S) and assess the potential contribution of hypothetical particles like the ATOMKI X17.

Method: Utilizing the Covariant Confined Quark Model, the study derives theoretical predictions for the hadronic form factor and decay constants.

Result: Predicted decay rates are Γ(Υ(1S)→η_bγ) = 9.3(9) eV and Γ(Υ(1S)→η_b e^+ e^-) = 4.8(5)×10^{-2} eV, with further modeling of the X17 vector boson's impact.

Conclusion: The findings affirm the efficacy of the Covariant Confined Quark Model in predicting Υ(1S) decay processes, while introducing the X17 as a potential factor in the Dalitz channel.

Abstract: We study the radiative decay $Υ(1S)\to η_bγ$ and the Dalitz decay $Υ(1S)\to η_b e^+ e^-$ within the framework of the Covariant Confined Quark Model.
