We provide theoretical predictions for the hadronic form factor, the radiative decay constant, and the decay branching fractions. Within the Standard Model we predict $Γ(Υ(1S)\toη_b γ) = 9.3(9)\, \textrm{eV}$ and $Γ(Υ(1S)\toη_b e^+ e^-) = 4.8(5)\times 10^{-2}\, \textrm{eV}$. The contribution of the hypothetical ATOMKI $X17$ vector boson to the Dalitz channel is also investigated.

</details>


### [95] [Quasicrystalline Inflation: From the Cosmological Dipole to Primordial Diffraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02142)
*Jiro Soda*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a model of quasicrystalline inflation, highlighting the significance of phonons and phasons in generating observable correlations linked to primordial quasiperiodic structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the effects of quasicrystalline structures in inflationary cosmology and their observational implications.

Method: Analyzing six quasiperiodic phases leading to local power dipole generation via coherent pure-phason displacements, employing effective field theory to derive off-diagonal correlations.

Result: The study finds that the momentum transfer in the correlated off-diagonal signals is restricted to integer combinations of six icosahedral wavevectors, indicating a distinct signature of quasiperiodicity.

Conclusion: These off-diagonal signals serve as a characteristic observational signature of primordial quasiperiodic order, termed primordial diffraction.

Abstract: We propose quasicrystalline inflation. Six quasiperiodic phases decompose into three phonons and three phasons. It turns out that a coherent pure-phason displacement can generate a local power dipole while the linear traceless quadrupole vanishes. From spatially varying quadratic effective field theory, we derive off-diagonal correlations whose momentum transfer is restricted to integer combinations of the six icosahedral wavevectors. These correlated off-diagonal signals provide a characteristic observational signature of primordial quasiperiodic order, primordial diffraction.

</details>


### [96] [Macroscopic Coherent Axion Production by Reverse Parametric Fluorescence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02150)
*Zhan Bai,Baifei Shen,Liangliang Ji,Ruxin Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a method to generate axion-like particles (ALPs) in a laboratory setup using optical modes and phase matching, significantly enhancing the detection of these particles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance laboratory searches for low-mass axion-like particles (ALPs) by improving detection sensitivity and generating these particles using realistic crystal and laser setups.

Method: Two counterpropagating optical modes induce reverse parametric fluorescence in a crystal, converting pump photons into ALPs via virtual ionic transitions, with coherent emission facilitated by phase matching.

Result: The production rate of ALPs is affected by pump powers and the source length, achieving a sensitivity reach of around g_{ae}  2.8  d7 10^{-11} with one year of operation, representing significant improvement.

Conclusion: This method provides a continuously tunable source of ALPs, greatly enhancing the sensitivity of laboratory-based searches for these particles.

Abstract: We propose a macroscopically coherent laboratory source of axion-like particles (ALPs) through the axion--electron coupling \(g_{ae}\). Two counterpropagating optical modes drive reverse parametric fluorescence in a crystal, where two pump photons are converted into a relativistic ALP through virtual ionic transitions, while the medium returns to its initial state. Phase matching enables emission amplitudes from many ions to add coherently without preparing material coherence. The pump frequencies determine the ALP energy, making the source continuously tunable. The production rate scales with the product of the two pump powers and the square of the source length. Resonant absorption followed by fluorescence completes the detection scheme. For benchmark crystal and laser parameters, a one-year operation gives a reach of \(g_{ae}\simeq2.8\times10^{-11}\), substantially improving the sensitivity of purely laboratory-based searches for low-mass ALPs.

</details>


### [97] [Evading Sudakov Dilution in Gluon Tomography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02280)
*Shuo Lin,Jian Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses a method to enhance gluon tomography precision by suppressing broadening effects through fiducial hadronic recoil, leading to improved measurements of azimuthal harmonics in deep inelastic scattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The precision of gluon tomography is limited by Sudakov broadening and competing azimuthal harmonics resulting from final-state soft radiation.

Method: The authors introduce a technique using fiducial hadronic recoil, which is the vector sum of hadronic transverse momenta within a rapidity interval, with a tagged jet to fix the azimuthal axis, minimizing the contributions to recoil broadening and azimuthal harmonics.

Result: Using this method, the $h_1^{\perp g}$ contribution is enhanced by a factor of 4.6, while the impact of final-state soft gluons is suppressed by a factor of 4.0 for a rapidity interval of $Δη=3$.

Conclusion: Fiducial recoil significantly improves precision in gluon-TMD tomography, allowing better measurements without needing a soft-radiation veto.

Abstract: Sudakov broadening and competing azimuthal harmonics from final-state soft radiation limit the precision of gluon tomography. We suppress both effects using fiducial hadronic recoil---the vector sum of all hadronic transverse momenta within a rapidity interval---while a tagged jet fixes the azimuthal axis. Momentum conservation ensures that emissions inside the interval contribute neither to recoil broadening nor to these harmonics, so widening the interval reduces their impact without a soft-radiation veto. As a benchmark, we study the $\cos 2φ$ modulation probing $h_1^{\perp g}$ in DIS. For rapidity interval $Δη=3$, fiducial recoil enhances the $h_1^{\perp g}$ contribution by a factor of $4.6$ and suppresses the final-state soft gluon term by a factor of $4.0$ relative to the conventional dijet imbalance. Fiducial recoil thereby enables precision gluon-TMD tomography.

</details>


### [98] [Quark masses and mixing in the D_5 model under spontaneous CP violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02312)
*Dong-Ping Fu,Michihisa Takeuchi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates CP violation within a four-Higgs model using D_5 symmetry, concluding that the model cannot account for observed CP violation due to a block-diagonal CKM matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore potential CP violation sources in the Yukawa sector of a four-Higgs model.

Method: Systematic analysis of quark field representation assignments under D_5 symmetry, imposing conditions on the CKM matrix and quark masses.

Result: Only one viable representation (one D_5 doublet and one singlet) yields two quark mass models that lead to block-diagonal CKM matrices, resulting in zero CP violation.

Conclusion: The D_5 4HDM under spontaneous CP violation cannot explain experimental observations due to structure leading to a vanishing CKM mixing.

Abstract: It is known that CP violation occurs in flavor physics, and the CKM matrix is complex. We investigate the Yukawa sector of a four-Higgs model based on D_5 symmetry. To understand all possible sources of CP violation in the Yukawa sector, we systematically analyze the impact of all possible representation assignments of the left-handed and right-handed quark fields under the D_5 group. After imposing the conditions of non-block-diagonal CKM matrix and the absence of massless quarks, we find that the only viable representation consists of one D_5 doublet and one singlet. In this case, two distinct types of quark mass models emerge in the quark sector. For each model, we introduce the most general vacuum that allows spontaneous CP violation (SCPV) into the mass matrices. Due to the specific structure of the D_5 symmetry, the vacuum expectation values (vevs) satisfy particular relations such that, for both quark models, the up- and down-quark mass squared matrices (i.e., M_d M_d^\dagger and M_u M_u^\dagger) can be simultaneously block-diagonalized by the same unitary transformation, forcing the CKM matrix to be block-diagonal and thus yielding vanishing mixing angles and CP violation. This result rules out the possibility of explaining experimental observations within the D_5 4HDM under the SCPV framework.

</details>


### [99] [Distribution amplitudes of vector and axial-vector mesons in a nonperturbatively improved symmetry-preserving framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02329)
*Zhen-Ni Xu,Zhao-Qian Yao,Peng Cheng,Khépani Raya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study predicts light-front distribution amplitudes (DAs) for various axial-vector mesons using continuum Schwinger methods, highlighting differences in polarization effects between vector mesons and axial-vector channels due to symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore light-front distribution amplitudes of specific mesons and their behavior under symmetry constraints, particularly in nonperturbative regimes.

Method: Continuum Schwinger-function methods with a nonperturbatively improved, symmetry-preserving kernel were used to reconstruct DAs from Bethe–Salpeter wave functions and analyze their properties across channels.

Result: Findings demonstrate that longitudinal and transverse DAs for vector mesons are nearly equal, while axial-vector mesons show distinct behavior under symmetry breaking, with specific charge conjugation constraints affecting their moments and projections.

Conclusion: The study concludes that the primary distinction between vector and axial-vector sectors arises from symmetry-enforced zeros rather than flavor asymmetry, highlighting the effects of symmetry breaking on coupling constants.

Abstract: Using continuum Schwinger-function methods with a nonperturbatively improved, symmetry-preserving kernel, we deliver predictions for the leading-twist light-front distribution amplitudes (DAs) of the $ρ$, $K^\ast$, $a_1(1260)$, $b_1(1235)$, and the unmixed strange partners of the $1^{++}$ and $1^{+-}$ axial-vector (AV) channels, reconstructed from Mellin moments of the associated Bethe--Salpeter wave functions. For vector mesons, polarisation barely affects longitudinal momentum sharing: the longitudinal and transverse DAs are nearly degenerate, and both narrower than the asymptotic distribution in the second-moment sense. The AV sector is different in kind. Charge conjugation compels one projection -- interchanged between the $1^{++}$ and $1^{+-}$ channels -- to vanish at $x=1/2$ and change sign; breaking $SU_F(3)$ symmetry removes this protection, whereupon the zeroth moments become nonzero and the nodes shift from the midpoint. Under a common weighted normalisation, the $1^{+-}$ zeroth moment is $1.63$ times that of the $1^{++}$ channel, and the profile distortion follows the same pattern. A coupling forbidden by charge conjugation in the symmetric limit, $f_{b_1}=0$, becomes $f_{K_1^{+-}}=0.019\,$GeV in the strange channel: an independent measure of the same symmetry breaking, obtained from a current matrix element rather than from the DA reconstruction. What distinguishes the two sectors is thus a symmetry-enforced zero, not the size of the flavour asymmetry.

</details>


### [100] [Strictly solvable $d=4$ quantum field theory models with unitarity violation. Possible implications for baryon asymmetry of the Universe and dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02383)
*N. V. Krasnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores quantum field theory with unitarity violation, presenting models that address baryon asymmetry and dark matter, suggesting dark matter particles with specific properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study seeks to understand phenomena beyond the standard model of particle physics, specifically related to baryon asymmetry and dark matter.

Method: Develops four-dimensional solvable models that incorporate unitarity violation to explain the universe's baryon asymmetry and dark matter.

Result: Presents two models where dark matter has nonzero baryon number and suggests that the total baryon number of the universe is zero with dark matter mass around 6 GeV.

Conclusion: The models predict low interaction cross-sections for dark matter, indicating that traditional underground experiments are unlikely to detect it.

Abstract: In this paper we consider
  quantum field theory with unitarity violation. We construct several $d = 4$ strictly solvable models with
  unitarity violation.
  As a possible application of the models with unitarity violation we
  construct two models explaining
  the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe and the dark matter existence.
  In considered models dark matter particles have nonzero baryon number and
  the total baryon number of the Universe being the sum of the baryon number of the matter and dark matter is equal to zero.
  Dark matter paricles have mass $\sim 6~GeV$. Proposed models predict negligible cross sections of the
  dark matter with nuclei and electrons. It means that underground experiments will fail to discover dark matter.

</details>


### [101] [Entanglement Signatures of Kinetic-Mixing Portals in Dark Monopole Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02467)
*Shilpa Jangid,Hiroshi Okada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum entanglement in particle-portal scattering involving Standard Model fermions and dark magnetic monopoles, using an information-theoretic approach to quantify quantum correlations and establish new observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quantum entanglement in the interaction between SM fermions and topological dark magnetic monopoles, extending beyond traditional phenomenological approaches.

Method: An information-theoretic approach is utilized, calculating Von Neumann entropy and subsystem purity deterioration to quantify quantum correlations during scattering events.

Result: The findings indicate that the dark monopole's non-perturbative core form factor influences high-energy momentum transfer, with Von Neumann entropy increasing quadratically with topological magnetic charge and kinetic-mixing parameter.

Conclusion: An information-theoretic framework is proposed to restrict portal parameters and investigate topological structures without relying solely on conventional energy signatures.

Abstract: We examine the interaction between the Standard Model (SM) fermions and the topological dark magnetic monopoles mediated via a kinetic-mixing portal to investigate the generation of quantum entanglement in particle-portal scattering. While the conventional phenomenology only takes into account total cross-sections, decay widths, and missing-energy signatures, we employ an information-theoretic approach to explain the scattering event. We quantify the quantum correlations transported over the portal boundary by calculating the Von Neumann entropy ($S_{\text{ent}}$) and the subsystem purity deterioration ($γ< 1$) analytically. Our results demonstrate that the non-perturbative core form factor of the dark monopole controls its high-energy momentum transfer and that the Von Neumann entropy increases quadratically with the topological magnetic charge ($g_m^2$) and the kinetic-mixing parameter ($ε^2$). This paradigm provides an information-theoretic "microscope" to restrict portal parameter spaces and explore underlying topological structures without using traditional energy signatures by establishing quantum decoherence and purity loss as new, complementary observables.

</details>


### [102] [Lepton-flavor violation and muon $(g-2)$ in the flavor-dependent $U(1)_F$ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02483)
*Yu-Ju Peng,Feng-Yan Niu,Qi-Zhen Qin,Zhan Cao,Jin-Lei Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores lepton flavor violation (LFV) processes and their connections to the muon anomalous magnetic dipole moment (MDM) within a framework extending the Standard Model using $U(1)_F$ local gauge symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LFV processes are typically forbidden in the Standard Model, so their potential observation would indicate new physics beyond it.

Method: The study investigates muon MDM and LFV processes using the $U(1)_F$ gauge symmetry, analyzing the contributions from new Yukawa couplings and their correlation with fermion flavor properties.

Result: The new interactions resulting from the FDM can significantly influence LFV processes, with predicted branching ratios potentially within the reach of future experimental sensitivity, especially concerning the processes $l_{j}^{-} \to l_{i}^{-} γ$ and $l_{j}^{-} \to l_{i}^{-}l_{i}^{-}l_{i}^{+}$.

Conclusion: The incorporation of $U(1)_F$ charges enhances our understanding of LFV processes and allows for precise predictions of their branching ratios in light of current experimental constraints.

Abstract: Lepton flavor violation (LFV) processes are forbidden in the standard model (SM), hence the observation of LFV transitions would represent a clear signal of new physics beyond the SM. In this work, we investigate the muon anomalous magnetic dipole moments (MDM) and LFV processes $l_{j}^{-}\to l_{i}^{-}γ$ and $l_{j}^{-}\to l_{i}^{-}l_{i}^{-}l_{i}^{+}$ in the extension of the SM with $U(1)_F$ local gauge symmetry (FDM). The muon anomalous MDM is one of the most precisely measured quantities in particle physics, which can be used to constrain the contributions from new couplings in the FDM. The newly incorporated $U(1)_F$ charges are correlated with the flavor properties of fermions, hence the newly added Yukawa couplings make contributions to these LFV processes. Considering the latest experimental constraints on the muon anomalous MDM, the new interactions in the FDM can make significant contributions to the LFV processes $l_{j}^{-}\to l_{i}^{-}γ$ and $l_{j}^{-}\to l_{i}^{-}l_{i}^{-}l_{i}^{+}$, the predicted branching ratios can well reach the future experimental sensitivity.

</details>


### [103] [Scalar Portal Verifiable Light Dark Matter and Correlated Gravitational Wave Signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02501)
*Ki-Young Choi,Erdenebulgan Lkhagvadorj,Satyabrata Mahapatra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a minimal scalar portal model with vector-like fermion dark matter, addressing challenges faced by Light Dark Matter (LDM) in achieving detectable interaction rates and relic density. It proposes a non-standard cosmological phase pre-Big Bang Nucleosynthesis and highlights observable gravitational wave signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to explore Light Dark Matter (LDM) due to the limitations of the WIMP paradigm and constraints from cosmic observations.

Method: A minimal scalar portal extension is introduced with a vector-like fermion dark matter candidate. The model invokes a stiff fluid during pre-BBN cosmology to enhance Hubble expansion, allowing for effective dark matter freeze-out and satisfying relic density requirements while evading constraints from the Cosmic Microwave Background (CMB).

Result: The model successfully connects the dark matter abundance and detection rates to observational signals, particularly highlighting a unique high-frequency tilt in gravitational waves due to the pre-BBN phase.

Conclusion: This framework underscores the potential for multi-messenger observations to concurrently investigate both the dark sector and the pre-BBN history of the Universe.

Abstract: The lack of signals in direct detection experiments has placed the canonical Weakly Interacting Massive Particle (WIMP) paradigm under severe tension, motivating a shift toward the sub-GeV Light Dark Matter (LDM) regime. However, realizing detectable LDM interaction rates typically requires large couplings to the visible sector, which leads to a severe thermal underabundance of the dark matter relic density within standard cosmology. Furthermore, LDM models featuring vector mediators face stringent constraints from the Cosmic Microwave Background (CMB) due to late-time energy injection. In this work, we propose a minimal scalar portal extension featuring a vector-like fermion dark matter candidate, which naturally evades CMB bounds via inherent p-wave annihilation suppression. To simultaneously achieve the correct relic density and large direct-detection couplings, we invoke a pre-Big Bang Nucleosynthesis (BBN) non-standard cosmology dominated by a stiff fluid ($w > 1/3$). The enhanced Hubble expansion during this epoch triggers an early dark matter freeze-out, successfully rescuing the asymptotic relic abundance. Crucially, this stiff pre-BBN phase heavily blue-shifts inflationary gravitational waves that re-enter the horizon prior to BBN, imprinting a distinct high-frequency tilt on the stochastic gravitational wave background. We establish a robust correlation between the non-standard expansion history, the particle physics parameters verifiable in future terrestrial direct detection experiments, and the unique gravitational wave signatures observable by forthcoming space-based interferometers like LISA and DECIGO. This framework highlights how multi-messenger observations can concurrently probe the dark sector and the pre-BBN thermal history of the Universe.

</details>


### [104] [Dark Matter at the Kinematic Edge: Interpreting the 248 keV LZ Nuclear-Recoil Candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02608)
*Mattia Di Mauro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ Collaboration's reported $248$ keV nuclear recoil event prompts an investigation into dark matter (DM)–nucleon interactions compatible with relic abundance and indirect-detection constraints. While elastic spin-independent scattering is inadequate for explaining the event, elastic spin-dependent and endothermic scattering mechanisms are viable. A thermal pseudo-Dirac fermion or thermal Higgsino could account for the observed recoil, with predictions for gamma-ray signals guiding future searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze the compatibility of the reported $248$ keV nuclear recoil event with dark matter models that satisfy relic abundance and indirect detection constraints.

Method: The paper evaluates elastic spin-independent and spin-dependent scattering and explores endothermic scattering mechanisms. It models a thermal pseudo-Dirac fermion and a thermal Higgsino to align predictions with the LZ event.

Result: Elastic spin-independent scattering fails to account for the high-energy recoil; however, endothermic and spin-dependent scattering models are viable. Specific predictions for relic density and potential gamma-ray signals are provided for both dark matter candidates.

Conclusion: Combining constraints from direct detection, relic density, and indirect detection significantly narrows the viable explanations for the LZ event and outlines specific predictions for future observational tests.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration recently reported one event consistent with a $248$ keV nuclear recoil in a $2.84$ tonne-year exposure, with a maximum local significance of $3.4σ$ and a global significance of $2.6σ$. We investigate whether the dark matter (DM)--nucleon interactions favored by this high-energy event can arise from particle DM models that simultaneously reproduce the observed relic abundance and satisfy indirect-detection constraints. Using the published LZ efficiency and operator significances, we show that elastic spin-independent (SI) scattering poorly explains an isolated high-energy recoil because its spectrum is concentrated at lower energies, whereas elastic spin-dependent (SD) $\mathcal{O}_4$ scattering remains viable. Endothermic scattering instead naturally suppresses the low-energy rate and shifts the recoil spectrum toward the observed energy. A thermal pseudo-Dirac fermion with an off-diagonal vector interaction provides a simple realization of this mechanism. For $m_χ\simeq1$ TeV, the relic-density requirement predicts $σ_N\simeq6.5\times10^{-43}\,\mathrm{cm}^2$, while a splitting $δ\simeq297$ keV shifts the recoil spectrum into the LZ event region. Present-day indirect-detection signals can be strongly suppressed because freeze-out proceeds mainly through coannihilation, while the excited state is depleted at late times. A thermal Higgsino provides a more predictive realization: its relic abundance fixes the mass near $1.1$ TeV, while a splitting $δ\simeq 371$ keV is required to reproduce the event. This interpretation is testable through the associated gamma-ray line signal. Overall, combining direct detection, relic density, and indirect detection significantly restricts the viable interpretations of the LZ event and provides concrete targets for future searches.

</details>


### [105] [Dark Matter as the Z_2 Partner of the Standard Model Higgs Boson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02505)
*Yasunori Nomura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses a gamma-ray excess linked to dark matter masses of hundreds of GeV, proposing a model that identifies dark matter as a scalar doublet correlated with a recent nuclear recoil event in an experiment, predicting measurable annual modulation in future observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observation of a gamma-ray excess from the Galactic halo suggests the need for a dark matter model that can explain its properties, particularly one that connects to recent experimental findings.

Method: The authors explore an electroweak scalar doublet model with a Z_2 symmetry, relating it to a nuclear-recoil event in the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment, and predict recoil outcomes based on dark matter mass ranges of 440-600 GeV.

Result: A mass-dependent splitting of neutral states near the kinematic boundary is identified, allowing for observed nuclear recoil events and suggesting a significant annual modulation effect.

Conclusion: Although the observations are not yet conclusive, the proposed model provides a clear framework to test the connection between dark matter and upcoming experimental results.

Abstract: A possible gamma-ray excess from the Galactic halo favors dark matter with a mass of several hundred GeV. In previous work, we found that an electroweak scalar doublet is a particularly economical, and nearly uniquely selected, interpretation among simple renormalizable thermal dark-matter models. We point out that its unbroken Z_2 symmetry is exactly equivalent to an interchange symmetry between two Higgs doublets, making dark matter the Z_2-odd partner of the Standard Model Higgs boson and suggesting that the two doublets may share a common weak-scale origin.
  We connect this model to the recently reported 248 keV nuclear-recoil event in the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment. For dark-matter masses of 440--600 GeV, a technically natural, mass-dependent neutral-state splitting of order 350 keV defines a narrow strip near the kinematic boundary where the fixed off-diagonal Z interaction can produce the observed recoil. Because the scattering probes the extreme high-velocity tail of the Galactic distribution, this interpretation predicts a large annual modulation, with future events preferentially occurring around early summer. Neither observation is yet conclusive, but their common interpretation is sharply testable.

</details>


### [106] [Analysis of the two-body strong decays of the hidden-charm pentaquark states in QCD sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02537)
*Zi-Hao Chen,Han-Xiong Huang,Jie Lu,Xiao-Song Yang,Zhi-Gang Wang,Guo-Liang Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study analyzes two-body strong decays of hidden-charm pentaquark states using QCD sum rules, identifying potential candidates and decay widths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the strong decays of hidden-charm pentaquark states and identify their relationship to experimentally observed states.

Method: Utilizes three-point QCD sum rules to derive decay channels and compute strong coupling constants; involves diquark-diquark-antiquark interpolating currents and operator product expansion.

Result: Identifies potential connections between compact hidden-charm pentaquark states and the observed $P_c(4312)$ and $P_c(4457)$, with predictions for other states.

Conclusion: Findings could assist in the identification of hidden-charm pentaquark states in experimental settings.

Abstract: In the present work, we study the two-body strong decays of the hidden-charm pentaquark states with the quark content $uudc\bar c$ and the quantum numbers $I(J^P)=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}^-)$ in the framework of the three-point QCD sum rules. The initial pentaquark states are described by four local diquark-diquark-antiquark type interpolating currents with definite isospin. We construct the three-point correlation functions for the decay channels $P_c\to η_c p$, $J/ψp$, $Λ_c\bar D$, $Λ_c\bar D^{*}$ and $Σ_c\bar D$, and derive the corresponding QCD sum rules for the strong coupling constants. At the hadron side, the correlation functions are expressed in terms of the hadron masses, pole residues, decay constants and strong coupling constants. At the QCD side, they are calculated by carrying out the operator product expansion with the full quark propagators, where the vacuum condensates up to dimension 10 are taken into account. After matching the two representations and performing the double Borel transformations, we extract the strong coupling constants from the selected Lorentz structures. With the obtained coupling constants, we evaluate the partial decay widths and discuss the possible assignments of the corresponding pentaquark states. The numerical results indicate that two of the compact hidden-charm pentaquark states can be related to the $P_c(4312)$ and $P_c(4457)$, respectively, while the other two lower-mass states may be regarded as possible hidden-charm pentaquark candidates to be searched for in future experiments. The present results may be useful for identifying the hidden-charm pentaquark states in future experiments.

</details>


### [107] [Inelastic Dark Photon Dark Matter for the LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event and the Galactic Halo Gamma-Ray Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02868)
*Kimiko Yamashita*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A dark-photon dark matter model explains both a Galactic gamma-ray excess and a nuclear-recoil event, using a light scalar mediator and an associated heavier vector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve two anomalies in particle astrophysics: the Galactic gamma-ray excess and a specific nuclear-recoil event from LUX-ZEPLIN.

Method: A $U(1)_X$ dark photon model with a mass of 420 GeV, incorporating a light scalar mediator to allow for inelastic transitions between the dark photon and a heavier vector partner.

Result: The model successfully accounts for the nuclear-recoil event by producing an inelastic transition that aligns with observed energy levels, while preserving previous phenomenological results for dark matter.

Conclusion: This framework provides a cohesive explanation for the two anomalies without disrupting existing models of relic abundance or dark matter halo structures.

Abstract: We show that a single dark-photon dark matter framework can simultaneously account for two recently reported anomalies: the halo-like Galactic gamma-ray excess identified by Totani in 15 years of Fermi-LAT data, and the $E_{\rm nr}=248\pm23\,({\rm stat})\pm23\,({\rm sys})$ keV nuclear-recoil event of interest reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN Collaboration. The dark matter is a $U(1)_X$ dark photon $X$ with mass $m_X=420$ GeV, stabilized by a dark parity that forbids kinetic mixing with the Standard Model photon. A light scalar mediator $φ$, already required to explain the Totani excess via Sommerfeld enhancement, is extended to couple a nearly degenerate heavier vector partner $V$ to $X$. The resulting inelastic transition $X+N \to V+N$, with mass splitting $δ\simeq316$~keV close to the kinematic endpoint for $m_X=420$ GeV, naturally reproduces the localized LZ event once the detector energy resolution is included, while leaving the relic abundance and the halo phenomenology of the original Totani-motivated benchmark essentially unchanged.

</details>


### [108] [$D\rightarrow π$ transitions from QCD Light-Cone Sum Rules with the chiral currents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02602)
*Di Gao,Jiangshan Lan,Duojie Jia,Xingbo Zhao,Yanjun Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study investigates the transition form factors of semileptonic D decays using QCD light-cone sum rules, emphasizing pion structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance understanding of semileptonic D meson decays and nonperturbative effects in QCD, particularly related to pion distribution amplitudes.

Method: Utilizes QCD light-cone sum rules with chiral currents, incorporating meson distribution amplitudes via Gegenbauer expansions and the Basis Light-Front Quantization method.

Result: Derived relations between transition form factors consistent with QCD chiral symmetry; predictions align with BESIII measurements and lattice QCD results.

Conclusion: The findings suggest precise extraction of CKM matrix elements and potential exploration of physics beyond the Standard Model, with significant agreement in differential decay widths for D-to-pion decay.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of the transition form factors for the semileptonic decays $D\rightarrow Pe^{+}v_{e}$ and $D\rightarrow Pμ^{+}\bar{v}_μ$ using QCD light-cone sum rules with chiral currents, with emphasis on the nonperturbative structure of the pion. The distribution amplitudes of the meson $π$, including twist-2 and higher-twist components, are analyzed and incorporated in our computation, employing both Gegenbauer polynomial expansions and the Basis Light-Front Quantization (BLFQ) method, where the latter yields a distribution that rapidly converges to the asymptotic form. Two relations linking transition form factors are derived and found to be consistent with the chiral symmetry of QCD. Our numerical predictions for the $D$-to-pion form factors agee well with BES\uppercase\expandafter{\romannumeral3} measurements and lattice QCD calculations. Furthermore, the differential decay widths obtained thereby for the $D$-to-pion decay also show consistency with the BES\uppercase\expandafter{\romannumeral3} data in the high-momentum-transfer ($q$) region with the $q^{2}>1\ \rm{GeV^2}$, implying new opportunities for the precise extraction of the CKM matrix element and the exploration of physics beyond the Standard Model.

</details>


### [109] [Entanglement entropy minimization and global symmetry violation in scatterings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02691)
*Shinya Kanemura,Masanori Tanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the relationship between quantum entanglement minimization in particle scattering and the suppression of symmetry-violating interactions, proposing that this minimization may help identify symmetry-preserving points amid new BSM interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the theoretical implications of quantum entanglement in particle interactions and its connection to symmetries in scattering processes.

Method: The paper investigates interactions beyond the Standard Model that violate global symmetries, analyzing how these interactions relate to the entanglement entropy generated in scattering events.

Result: It is shown that symmetry-violating interactions can lead to final-state sectors that are orthogonal to the symmetry-preserving sector, making the latter a local minimum of entanglement entropy.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that minimizing entanglement may serve as a significant information-theoretic principle, aiding in the understanding of BSM phenomena related to symmetry violations.

Abstract: The concept of quantum information has provided new ways to investigate the theoretical structure of particle interactions. In particular, it has been shown that extrema of quantum entanglement in scattering and decay processes can be related to symmetries and characteristic properties of particles. We focus on a more specific question: whether the minimization of entanglement entropy generated in scattering processes is systematically related to the suppression of interactions that violate global symmetries or selection rules. We conjecture that the minimization of the entanglement entropy can select the symmetry-preserving point when a new interaction opens a symmetry-violating scattering channel. We investigate the plausibility of this conjecture by considering interactions beyond the Standard Model (BSM) that violate global symmetries or the corresponding selection rules, including lepton number violation, baryon number violation, lepton flavor violation, and flavor-changing neutral currents. We show that, when symmetry-violating interactions open final-state sectors that are orthogonal to the symmetry-preserving sector, the symmetry-preserving point becomes a local minimum of the entanglement entropy. Our results support the possibility that the minimization of entanglement provides a common information-theoretic principle underlying the suppression of BSM phenomena relevant to symmetry-violating interactions.

</details>


### [110] [Next-to-Leading-Order Multi-Jet Merging of Higgs Boson Production via Vector Boson Fusion in the Presence of Anomalous Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02758)
*Tinghua Chen,Terrance Figy,Tunde Kushimo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study simulates Higgs boson production through vector boson fusion at the LHC, focusing on anomalous couplings and utilizing advanced event generation techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding Higgs boson production mechanisms with a focus on anomalous couplings to improve theoretical predictions and experimental analyses at the LHC.

Method: The study employs the Herwig event generator interfaced with VBFNLO, using a multi-jet merging scheme to combine NLO and LO matrix elements for various jet multiplicities, while investigating different matching/merging setups.

Result: The analysis reveals that azimuthal angle observables remain robust against QCD radiative corrections across the applied frameworks, providing insights into systematic uncertainties in Higgs production.

Conclusion: The findings enhance the precision of theoretical predictions for Higgs boson production via VBF in the presence of anomalous couplings, contributing to better understanding and analysis in experimental settings.

Abstract: We present the results of a detailed simulation study of Higgs boson production via vector boson fusion (VBF) at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) in the presence of anomalous couplings. The analysis utilizes the Herwig event generator interfaced with VBFNLO, employing a multi-jet merging scheme that combines NLO accurate matrix elements for Higgs plus 2- and 3-jet multiplicities with LO accurate matrix elements for Higgs plus 4-jet multiplicities. We investigate both matching and merging setups to consistently combine these hard scattering processes with QCD parton showers. Finally, we demonstrate the robustness of azimuthal angle observables against QCD radiative corrections across these frameworks.

</details>


### [111] [Next-to-leading order QCD corrections to fully charm tetraquark hadronic decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02761)
*Yefan Wang,Fengxiang Zhao,Ruilin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study calculates NLO QCD corrections for light hadron decays of fully charm tetraquarks, revealing significant impacts on predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions for fully charm tetraquark decays in light of new experimental opportunities.

Method: Utilized the NRQCD factorization framework to derive short-distance coefficients for $gg$ and $q\bar{q}$ states and calculated NLO corrections.

Result: NLO corrections alter LO predictions by approximately 170% for the $0^{++}$ state and 30% for the $2^{++}$ state, with $	ext{R}_{	ext{LH}}$ values much higher than diphoton decay rates.

Conclusion: The findings offer updated theoretical insights which are crucial for upcoming experimental investigations into fully charm tetraquark decays.

Abstract: We compute the next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD corrections to the light hadron decays of fully charm tetraquarks within the nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization framework. The short-distance coefficients for the $gg$ and $q\bar{q}$ final states from fully charm tetraquarks are obtained analytically. The NLO corrections are found to be significant, altering the LO predictions by about $170\%$ for the $0^{++}$ state and $30\%$ for the $2^{++}$ state. The resulting $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{LH}}$ values are of order $10^{-5}$ to $10^{-4}$ MeV, about three orders of magnitude larger than the diphoton channel, as expected for strong-interaction decays. Our results provide updated theoretical predictions for future experimental studies of fully charm tetraquark decays.

</details>


### [112] [The $H^*H^*V$ charge couplings from light-cone sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02791)
*Chao Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper provides an updated determination of strong charge couplings using light-cone sum rules, enhancing theoretical precision and stability in predictions despite uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the determination of strong charge couplings between heavy mesons and vector mesons, addressing theoretical uncertainties.

Method: Utilizing light-cone sum rules (LCSR) with next-to-leading-order (NLO) corrections and systematic power-suppressed contributions up to next-to-next-to-leading power (NNLP).

Result: A universal static coupling of β = 0.73 ± 0.13 is extracted, with stability in Borel plateaus and reduced factorization-scale dependence at NLO.

Conclusion: Charge couplings are largely insensitive to heavy-quark mass effects, with flavor symmetry breaking having negligible impact relative to uncertainties in vector meson distribution amplitudes.

Abstract: We present an updated and rigorous determination of the strong charge couplings $g_{H^*H^*V}$ (with $H \in \{D, B\}$ and $V \in \{ρ, ω, K^*, φ\}$) using the framework of light-cone sum rules (LCSR). The theoretical precision is significantly enhanced by establishing the leading-power hard-collinear factorization formula with next-to-leading-order (NLO) $α_s$ corrections, alongside the systematic inclusion of power-suppressed contributions up to the next-to-next-to-leading power (NNLP). Our numerical analysis demonstrates a subtle cancellation of the factorization-scale dependence at NLO and reveals highly stable Borel plateaus, yielding robust predictions for the couplings. By parameterizing the $\mathcal{O}(1/m_{H^*})$ power corrections, we extract the universal static coupling $β= 0.73 \pm 0.13$ and demonstrate that these charge couplings are remarkably insensitive to heavy-quark mass breaking effects. Finally, our investigation into SU(3) flavor symmetry breaking shows that its minute physical effects are currently overshadowed by uncertainties in the non-perturbative vector meson distribution amplitudes.

</details>


### [113] [MadSpin2: automated spin-entangled decays of heavy resonances via the spin-density matrix formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02794)
*Spyros Argyropoulos,Valentin Durupt,Olivier Mattelaer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: MADSPIN2 is an advanced tool for simulating multi-particle processes from unstable resonances, enhancing computational efficiency and expanding applicability through new methodologies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the computational challenges in simulating multi-particle decay processes from unstable resonances in high-energy physics.

Method: MADSPIN2 builds on the original MADSPIN, implementing spin-density matrices, supporting loop-induced processes, N-body decays, and using techniques like multiple-pole approximation and resonance-helicity decomposition.

Result: The new version demonstrates significant improvements in computational capabilities and the ability to handle previously inaccessible applications, showcased through several examples.

Conclusion: MADSPIN2 effectively broadens the phenomenological scope for simulations in particle physics, tackling challenges of multi-particle decay processes.

Abstract: The simulation of processes that contain multiple particles from the decay of unstable resonances becomes computationally challenging both at leading order and beyond. This paper presents MADSPIN2, a new version of MADSPIN, a dedicated tool tackling this problem, primarily for events generated with the MG5AMC event generator. While still based on the Frixione-Laenen-Motylinski-Webber formalism, the new version leverages spin-density matrices to lift most of the limitations of the previous implementation. It supports loop-induced processes, N -body decays and samples defined by amplitude-level interference between distinct hard-process amplitudes. It also introduces the multiple-pole approximation and a resonance-helicity decomposition, including off-diagonal helicity-interference contributions. These developments substantially broaden the phenomenological scope of MADSPIN, enabling applications that were previously inaccessible within the framework, several of which are demonstrated in this work.

</details>


### [114] [A Peccei--Quinn Origin for Inelastic Electroweak Dark Matter after LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02807)
*Luca Visinelli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LUX-ZEPLIN experiment reports a nuclear recoil event, interpreted through an inelastic electroweak doublet framework involving Peccei–Quinn symmetry and a colored sector providing the QCD anomaly. The model predicts dark matter components and suggests parameters for detectable signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain a nuclear recoil event in the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment using an inelastic electroweak doublet and Peccei–Quinn symmetry, addressing dark matter constituents and interaction mechanisms.

Method: Investigating a model where a PQ-charged singlet fermion generates Majorana mass and induces a dimension-five operator that splits a vectorlike EW doublet, incorporating a minimal KSVZ colored sector for the QCD anomaly.

Result: Derives a suitable relation between the Majorana mass and PQ-breaking scale, yielding estimates for dark matter parameters consistent with LUX-ZEPLIN data, including mass ranges and event counts for expected signals.

Conclusion: The framework accounts for a nuclear recoil signal while fitting predictions for dark matter composition and interaction, with additional luminous signals proposed from upscattering events.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has reported one nuclear recoil event reconstructed at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$\,keV in a search extending to $E_R\simeq270$\,keV. We investigate an inelastic electroweak (EW) doublet interpretation in which spontaneous Peccei--Quinn (PQ) breaking leaves a residual parity that stabilizes the lighter neutral state and generates the Majorana splitting. A PQ-charged singlet fermion acquires its Majorana mass from the PQ-breaking vacuum expectation value and, after being integrated out, induces the dimension-five operator that splits a vectorlike EW doublet. A minimal KSVZ colored sector supplies the QCD anomaly. For a representative PQ-breaking scale $v_S=4.4\times10^{11}$\,GeV and Majorana mass $M_N\simeq50$\,TeV, the relation $M_N=y_Sv_S/\sqrt{2}$ gives $y_S\simeq1.6\times10^{-7}$, while the splitting relevant for LZ requires a Higgs--doublet Yukawa coupling $y\simeq0.023$. In a standard thermal history, the EW doublet constitutes only a subcomponent of the dark matter. A rate estimate probing the high velocity tail gives representative benchmarks with $M_D\simeq380$--$420$\,GeV, $\dchi\simeq323$--$330$\,keV, and $ξ_χ\simeq0.12$--$0.15$ yielding an order-one LZ event count. The remaining abundance is supplied by the QCD axion through vacuum misalignment for suitable $v_S$ and initial angle. The excited state has a radiative lifetime of order $3\times10^{-2}$\,s and a decay length of order $20$\,km, leaving the LZ topology as a single nuclear recoil while enabling a complementary luminous signal after terrestrial upscattering.

</details>
